## Recorder stone

Recorder stones, also known as sensory stones, were a variety of enchanted gems unique to the Society of Sensation planar faction of Sigil.

Description
These stones were small, round in shape, and made of many semiprecious minerals.

Powers
Recorder stones allowed people to encode or "record" upon them their experiences, in the form of single or set of sensations. It usually took about an hour to record an experience, though long or complex ones could take an entire day to encode.

Once an experience was encoded upon a recorder stone, it could be accessed and experienced by anyone just by activating the stone, which required physically touching the stone and focus one's thoughts, though it was possible to enchant a recorder stone to only provide access to a specific user. Once a stone was activated, its user would lose all sense of reality and become engulfed in the sensation(s) of the stone.

Generally, whenever a recorder stone was removed from the sensoriums of the Civic Festhall, they would revert to unenchanted rock. Though there were some exceptions to this. In addition, it was possible to become addicted to the experiences provided by recorder stones.

History
In, Ayryn Farlight held a scrying session for select members of the Society of Sensation, using her sorcerous gift to observe and record activities of various deities of Toril on to recorder stones. When she attempted to scry the goddess of luck Tymora. Her magic sight was redirected by the goddess to observing Jasmine and Joel of Finder Wyvernspur's Band. Eventually, her sight was focused on Milil who was delighted to have an audience. The deity used magic to affix the scrying and performed the following songs: the Baker's Daughter, a jaunty hin tune - Pipeweed Dreams, a lengthy ballad - the Seven Sisters, followed by Three Thayvian Roses, the Purple Dragons of Cormyr, and a number of Faerunian folk songs about such mundane things as milking cows, weather, and crops. Eventually, the Sensates started getting bored of the performance, to what Milil responded by starting to sing an opera - the Fall of Myth Drannor. The long and torturous performance was a punishment for trying to record activities of gods. The entirety of this event, like all scrying gatherings of the Sensates, was ultimately encoded upon recorder stones.

Notable Users
Groups
 The Society of Sensation had millions of sensory stones stored away within their Civic Festhall on Sigil, containing experiences from people all across the planes, including those of devas, humans, fiends, and tieflings. Entry into their faction required contributing five worthwhile experiences to recorder stones, each focusing on a different sense, or one substantial experience that featured all five senses to some extent. They were also willing to pay 500 gold pieces for any sensations not already in their collection, especially anything they could use against the Fated and their factol, Duke Rowan Darkwood. These and all other recorder stones were kept within two grand halls of the Civic Festhall—the Sensate Sensorium, which was only accessible to members, and the Public Sensorium, which was open to the public. In the latter, individuals or groups could pay a fee of 100 gold pieces to partake in a specific sensation or experience, three times per day for up to four hours each.

Individuals
 Fall-from-Grace, a succubus and Sensate in the city of Sigil, operated a brothel that provided its working women sensory stones for use as diaries. These stones were all kept in one room and attuned to their individual users, making it impossible for others to access the experiences that they contained.
 The Nameless One, an amnesiac immortal being, used the Civic Festhall's sensory stones to help him rediscover his forgotten memories. He later returned to the Civic Festhall and recorded all the experiences he had gone through in the meantime, including the Faction War.
 Phineas, a member of the Society of Sensation, traveled to the city of Samargol on the Prime world of Toril in 1374 DR in search of exotic resources. When a group of adventurers managed to achieve all the resources he was after, Phineas gifted them the Namless One's sensory stone, having somehow not lost its enchantment despite its removal from the Civic Festhall.

## Rebinding

Rebinding was the rarest and perhaps most powerful spell known to hedge wizards; it rebuilt a destroyed magical item. Less than one in a thousand hedge mages knew this spell; it was known to some artificers.

Effects
The spell had to be cast once upon the broken item, prior to a mundane repair. After the mundane repair, the spell was recast; if it succeeded, there was a chance that the magical item became completely restored. The most definitive sources placed the chance at roughly 50% at every turn, improving for the most drastically powerful objects.

Components
The spell called for verbal and somatic components; as a material component, about 50 gold pieces worth of silver skein and two lodestones were needed.

Appendix
References

---

## Reaver (tool)

A reaver was a farm implement that could be used as a melee weapon.

Description
A reaver was designed to reduce the time and labor of harvesting in the fields. It consisted of two curved blades hinged at their tips. When swung, the blades extended until they hit something, at which point they folded at the hinge to scissor together. This scissoring action was known to make a distinctive whirling and scraping sound when used in the fields.

Weaponology
When used in combat, a wielder ran the risk of injuring themselves. This risk was offset, however, by the reaver's remarkably dangerous ability to easily decapitate a foe with a single strike.

Reputation
Some farmers swore that the use of reavers halved the time it took for them to complete their harvests.

## Re's Redemption

Re's Redemption were enchanted Greataxes created by the Mulhorandi deity Re.

Powers
These axes possessed a potent +2 enchantment. When wielded against orc and orc-kin, Re's Redemption effectively became a powerful +4 magic weapon. Additionally, when such axe landed a successful cleave on an evil creature, it dealt additional lightning damage.

History
The history of these powerful weapons began where their creator's story ended. During the calamitous Mulhorandi Orcgate Wars, the avatars of the Mulhorandi pantheon stood on the nation's soil and fought against the horde. Re was killed in the conflict, and with his dying breath, he created hundreds of enchanted great axes. Each weapon was dedicated to slaying orcs, and the great horde was forced northward with their aid. These weapons became known as Re's Redemption.

As of the, one such axe belonged to dwarf member of the Neverwinter Nine– Callum.

## Reef Street

Reef Street was a beautiful residential street in Khaerbaal, a port city of Halruaa.

Description
Reef Street was built far inland away from the shores of the Great Sea. Despite that fact, the street strongly smelled of the sea. Numerous aqueducts were erected along the street, carrying seawater via wondrous water pumps built by Khaerbaal's artificer guild. All buildings on the street belonged to the upper and middle-class citizens of the city. Buildings on Reef Street were molded from living corals through time, money, effort, and magic. The buildings' color varied from sandy pink to a deep rose.

Each coral building was slowly grown over the course of several years. The first thing placed was the building's "skeleton," made out of magically reinforced wood. Next, water was pumped on the top of the building frame, along with a small portal that attracted tiny coral creatures. They were tricked into building corals on the "skeleton" frame. The forming of coral started at the top and moved down. As the building was created, a cistern of water at the bottom of the frame collected trickling seawater and pumped it back up. once the building was complete, cistern was removed.

Corals were not the only aquatic feature of Reef Street. Walls and iron fences were decorated in wave-like marine designs. Abundant topiaries were trimmed in shapes of various fish, merfolk, and other aquatic creatures. One of the bigger shops on the street had massive gates that were carved depicting a duo of intimidating-looking sahuagin wielding tridents.

Another shop with a sea-green awning found on Reef Street dealt in expensive enchanted weapons. Each item was protected by numerous dweomers that warded the store's goods from theft.

A big walled public park stood on Reef Street with a massive bilboa tree that dwarfed most buildings on it and on the neighboring Sultan Street.

## Reed staff

Reed staff was a divine magic spell given to priests of Sheela Peryroyl, the Green Sister, and Yondalla, the Nurturing Matriarch. It temporarily turned a blade of grass into a quarterstaff.

Effects
This spell turned an ordinary blade of grass into a magical +0 quarterstaff that only the caster could use. If another being took possession or attempted to use it, the weapon was dispelled. Likewise, the reed staff could be destroyed by a successful dispel magic cast upon it, or touching an anti-magic shell or similar effect inimical to magic. The spell lasted for a minimum of four minutes (longer for more experienced casters) and the caster was not required to remain in contact with the quarterstaff once it had been created.

Components
This spell required a verbal command, the priest's holy symbol, a sliver of wood, and a blade of grass.

## Red Dragon Mask

The Red Dragon Mask was one of the five dragon masks that carried the blessings of Tiamat in the efforts of the reformed Cult of the Dragon to summon her to Toril. It was worn by the cult's leader, Severin Silrajin for a time during the 1480s DR.

Description
The Red Dragon Mask was a lustrous, crimson color and featured the archetypical rear-facing horns and ridge spikes associated with red dragons.

Powers
The wearer of the Red Dragon Mask gained resistance to any any harm caused by fire, along with protection from physical harm. Whenever they struck a creature or ignitable object with flame-related magic or any incendiary weapons, their target would catch aflame. The wearer was also granted darkvision and blindsight, along with an the understanding of the Draconic language.

The main feature of the Red Dragon Mask, along with the other dragon masks, was to transform its wearer into a wyrmspeaker. These individuals were granted thought processes similar to those of dragons, and granted slight influence over their behavior.

When two or more of the masks were brought together, they reformed as the Mask of the Dragon Queen, which combined their respective properties and granted them each upon the wearer. This valuable artifact was the essential piece needed to complete the ritual to free Tiamat from the Nine Hells.

History
As of the 1480s DR, the Red Dragon Mask was believed to be found somewhere in Icewind Dale. The Red Wizard Rath Modar led the search for the mask on behalf of the Cult of the Dragon, and of course his own aims, but his efforts were thwarted by a band of adventurers directed by five of the most powerful factions of the Sword Coast.

It was finally uncovered on the isle of Tuern, in the Trackless Sea, and taken by the dragon cult's leader, Severin Silrajin. He took the powerful artifact back with him to the Well of Dragons, the site where he planned to summon Tiamat from the Nine Hells.

## Redfeyer

Redfeyer was a singer and entertainer from the city of Rassatan in Samarach, active in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Redfeyer was a human male who wore brightly-colored clothes.

History
In the, Redfeyer made a living singing and performing for the folk of Rassatan. One day he heard a rumor of a strangely mesmerizing and beautiful Singing Caverns east of Rassatan and a treasure hidden in its depths. Redfeyer traveled along the Sanrach Mountains but found the caverns to be a perilous lair of giant spiders and angry bats. Once inside, Redfeyer's magical pendant, used to harmonize with during performances – the singing pendant, started doing just that, sing. The music attracted the hungry inhabitants of the cave, and Redfeyer fled with numerous bites, scratches, and wounds, losing his prized necklace in the process.

Disheartened by the loss of his beloved item he lovingly referred to as "she", Redfeyer was drinking his sadness away in the Forgotten Pearl tavern in Samargol. The singer hoped to find an able-bodied adventure willing to venture into the Singing Caverns and retrieve the pendant. Redfeyer offered the cavern's alleged treasure as a payment to those brave enough to help.

## Red wolf of Payit

Red wolves of Payit were a species of wolves or wolf-like creatures native to the continent of Maztica, lands surrounding the town of Helmsport.

Description
Red wolves of Payit resembled dire wolves of Faerûn but were slightly smaller in size and had reddish fur. Those who faced red wolves in battle said that the creatures' eyes gleamed with malevolent intelligence. This unsettling gleam remained present even after the creature was stuffed and preserved for display.

Ecology
These creatures demonstrated remarkable cunning, and it led to speculations that red wolves of Payit were cross-breeds of wolves and foxes. However, there were no known crossbreeds between the two species, the fact that led to sages to believe red wolves were a completely separate species.

History
Red wolves of Payit were first encountered by Amnian explorers in the Maztican nation of Payit when the continent was being "discovered", following the armed conflict of 1361 DR and 1362 DR.

Red wolves became a major threat to the settlers of Helmsport. Regular attacks took the lives of over a dozen builders, leading the colonial authorities to assemble a group to hunt down and kill the leader of the pack. The hunters succeeded, and the dead red wolf pack leader was stuffed and proudly displayed in Helmsport's Temple of Helm. The stuffed creature became known as the Red Wolf of Payit. The stuffing and display enraged the locals, who thought it to be disrespectful to the beast.

The killing of the pack leader stopped the red wolves' attacks on Helmsport. However, soon after, a chain of maladies afflicted the temple's inhabitants. Local Payits believed that the Red Wolf's spirit was haunting the temple, exacting revenge on its killers. Helmites and settlers considered it to be nothing but superstition. Their opinions started to waver after the temple's High Priest perished of a heartstop by the stuffed Red Wolf of Payit. After some unrest, the stuffed animal was removed and sent to Amn to calm the superstitious.

The Curse of the Red Wolf persisted, taking the lives of several owners of the stuffed beast in Amn. All were Helmites, and all died under mysterious circumstances. By the, the infamous stuffed wolf was accepted by planeswalking wizard of Westgate, Mintassan the Magnificent and displayed in his curio shop – Mintassan's Mysteries.

## Red Boar Inn

The Red Boar Inn, also known as the Red Boar Tavern, was of course an inn and tavern in the city of Loudwater in the Delimbiyr Vale in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. It was owned by the Rein family.

Location
It stood in the Low Town area of Loudwater, which was the predominantly human part of town, amongst coster headquarters, warehouses, craft guild houses, and lesser, seedier taverns. It was the most well-known inn there.

Interior
The common room was quite spacious. Within it was a large oaken pillar that was frequently used by Loudwater's human community as a message board, to notify others of events and proposals, to spread news, to share politics, to advertise jobs, and to call for adventurers and mercenaries. Though originally fairly plain, it came to be shrouded with decades' worth of papers and parchments.

Defenses
Owing to incidents around the Red Boar Tavern, the Loudwater Guard kept guards on duty there, with two in the day and four at night.

Atmosphere
Merchants and coster masters often met here to conduct business, as did those seeking employment with them, including adventurers and mercenaries. Anyone looking for work in and around Loudwater would be wise to start here. There was much talk about business, current affairs, and gossip, though patrons tended to avoid sensitive or controversial subjects. A hothouse of news and job-hunting, it was described as more like a market than an inn, but there was often much merrymaking at well, with the mercantile and political negotiating taking place in its more secluded corners.

The clientele were largely humans, halflings, and dwarves, as well as a few half-orcs. Elves and half-elves here were invariably adventurers and travelers from other lands, as local elves shunned the Red Boar as a symbol of human insensitivity and small-mindedness. The patrons in turn avoided discussing the origin of the oaken pillar with any elves, half-elves, and even gnomes who did visit. Orcs were not served at all until the Decree of Reception required them to, and then only grudgingly.

It was intensely busy at nights, with business peaking in the few hours after sunset.

The Red Boar was a typical stop on the 'Low Town ale quest', wherein young adventurers would sample a drink at every tavern and inn in the area and see how far they got.

Services
It provided ample food and drink to patrons in its common room.

History
The Red Boar was built by the first members of the Rein family to settle in Loudwater in the 1310s DR. For lumber, they cut down a massive oak tree that had been growing on the site—a tragic act that would anger the local elves for decades to come. A remnant of this oak was the main pillar in the common room. Not long after it was built, the pillar came to be used as a community notice board.

In the early 1370s DR, it was owned, though not managed, by Deldron Rein.

One summer evening the, only two bells after sunset, a pair of immigrant High Forest orcs were slain outside the Red Boar Tavern, either in the street or in the alley behind. Though this was the busiest time for the tavern, somehow, the authorities could not find one witness to the incident, let alone the killer. In response, High Lord Kalahar Twohands made the Decree of Reception to confirm the rights of the visiting orcs and ordered guards be posted to the Red Boar at all times to ensure peace there.

## Red Boar Trading Coster

The Red Boar Trading Coster was a coster based in the city of Loudwater in the Delimbiyr Vale in the late 14 century DR. It was led by Deldron Rein of the Rein family.

Organization
For a trading coster, the Red Boars had a significant guard—practically a private army. It was led by Deldron's son, Stedd Rein.

One of their most preferred caravan captains was Geveral Telkku.

Its employees wore their own livery.

Activities
The Rein family owned a pair of coasters that operated between Waterdeep and Baldur's Gate.

History
When his son Stedd returned from the College of War in Athkatla in the, while he was still en route to Loudwater, Deldron departed the city to greet his son and conduct business in Daggerford, taking the Red Boar Trading Coster's sizeable guard with him. But a magical communication from Loudwater alerted Deldron about a vicious lycanthrope attack, known as the Night of the Blood Moon, and the Reins and their Red Boar guard hastened to return. Deldron sent Stedd ahead with half his forces while he followed close behind.

Stedd and the Red Boar guard arrived in Loudwater on the third day of the conflict, linking with Gauntlets Kalahar Twohands and Harazos Thelbrimm. Twohands and Thelbrimm divided the city into quadrants for defense by their own armies, the Red Boar guard, and the elven house guards. Stedd opened the Red Boar's stores and equipped his forces with nearly forty alchemical silver weapons. Then, on the third night, Stedd Rein rallied his Red Boar forces and charged desperately through the Malarites' ranks, fighting all the way with silver blades and battle spells, to reach Standing Stone Hill in an effort to rescue the Green Regent Galaer Grasswave. It was a valiant effort, but too far and too hard to be successful. For his courage and leadership, Stedd Rein earned praise and accolades.

## Rein

The Rein family were a wealthy and controversial family based in the city of Loudwater in the Delimbiyr Vale in the 14 century DR. They were owners of the Red Boar Inn, Red Boar Trading Coster, and other ventures in the Delimbiyr Vale.

History
Hailing from Waterdeep, the Rein family settled in Loudwater in the 1310s DR and there they built the Red Boar Inn. For lumber, they cut down a massive oak tree that had been growing on the site—a tragic act that would anger the local elves for decades to come. Nevertheless, a remnant of this oak was used as the main pillar in the common room and not long after it was built, the pillar came to be used as a community notice board.Key to Phantoms' Cloister and Rat's Bastard say the Reins felled the oak "a century or so ago" from a setting date of 1372 DR, but the Legacy of the Green Regent background article "A Fellowship Rises" says this was "almost sixty winters ago". The later date is presumed here, as it is more exact and "a century or so" could conceivably encompass 60 years.

The Reins would prosper and later led the Red Boar Trading Coster.

In the summer of the, the people of the neighboring town of Orlbar revolted against the tyrannical rule of the Zhentarim that had seized control of their town, and they were backed by Loudwater. But Jesym Rein turned traitor to both the Orlbar rebels and the Loudwater soldiers, and thanks to her, the Zhentarim and clerics of Iyachtu Xvim brutally crushed the rebellion and Loudwater was forced to sign the Oath of Orlbar, a peace treaty that marked a border between the two states in the years after.  Her actions tarnished the Rein name with a reputation for treachery.

However, their reputation was soon healed in only the autumn of that year. At that time, Stedd Rein returned from the College of War in Athkatla, and while he was still en route to Loudwater, Deldron departed the city to greet his son and conduct business in Daggerford, taking the Red Boar Trading Coster's sizeable guard with him. But a magical communication from Loudwater alerted Deldron about a vicious lycanthrope attack, known as the Night of the Blood Moon, and the Reins and their Red Boar guard hastened to return. Arriving in time to aid in the third day of fighting, Stedd Rein displayed courage and leadership and his actions saved many lives, and he earned praise and accolades. It improved the Rein name, erasing the earlier memory of Jesym's treachery and earning them the support of the Beutaleen'dal elven house.

Possessions
The Reins continued to own the Red Boar Inn, but left it to be managed by others.

The Rein family owned a pair of coasters that operated between Waterdeep and Baldur's Gate.

Relationships
Despite Stedd's heroism, Jesym's betrayal was not forgotten, and in the early 1370s DR there were still some in Loudwater who believed the Reins would ultimately prove treacherous and must be kept an eye on.  The nine great elven families of Loudwater in particular typically opposed anything to do with the Reins, though the Beutaleen'dals eventually supported them.

Description
Some thought the Reins had "great fashion sense".

Notable Members
 Deldron Rein, family patriarch circa 1370 DR, owner of the Red Boar Inn and Red Boar Trading Coster.
 Jesym Rein, a traitor to Loudwater, a hero to the Zhentarim and followers of Iyachtu Xvim.
 Stedd Rein, hero of the Night of the Blood Moon.

## Renewed youth

Renewed youth was a spell used by clerics of Labelas Enoreth.

Effects
When cast, this spell restored the caster or a targeted creature to their peak physical condition of their prime. The spell was only effective on a recipient who was middle-aged or older. By means of this spell, the negative effects of aging were temporarily reversed. It also occasionally increased the recipients pain tolerance.

Weight of years was the reverse of this spell. This spell temporarily increased the age of a targeted creature. For the spell to be cast successfully, the caster had to touch the targeted creature. Weight of years could not kill the recipient of old age.

Both spells were useless against dragons and creatures from another plane or that were conjured.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of Labelas Enoreth and a fresh flower. The reverse required the holy symbol and an air dried flower or fruit that was crushed.

## Red ache

Red ache was a disease that caused the skin to become inflamed and the body to become weakened.

Acquisition
The disease could be acquired after sustaining an injury from an infected creature or following exposure to infected blood. It could potentially also be contracted from injuries received from rusty metal.

Known carriers included jungle landwyrms, plague ants, undead flies, and giant leeches. It could also be inflicted via the contagion, contagious touch, epidemic, and plague cloud spells.

Effects
Red ache was a corruption of the blood that caused the infected's skin to become swollen, red, and hot. It subsequently led to a steady loss of physical strength, possibly leading to death if not treated. Even post-mortem, the effects of red ache prevented the skin from decaying properly, with it remaining red, warm, and swollen. A deceased could still appear alive on casual inspection, even if infested with maggots within.

Curing
Red ache was treatable and ultimately curable, either on one's own or especially with the help of a talented healer.

Prolonged exposure to the red ache could eventually allow an individual to develop an immunity to it.

History
Red ache could be encountered in the Underdark.

Red ache was one of many diseases inflicted on the soldiers fighting in the Rotting War at the Fields of Nun in . Centuries later, some roving undead still carried the disease.

The Brotherhood of the Scarlet Scourge, a monastic order dedicated to the orc god of disease, Yurtrus, was known to weaponize the red ache against its enemies.

## Revenge

The Revenge was one of two ships constructed to begin the rebuilding of Zhentil Keep's navy following the Fall of Zhentil Keep in the .

History
Gondsmen were hired for a secret project during the Revenge's construction. The Revenge first sailed in Marpenoth of the . Later in Uktar of that same year it encountered five hostile ships from the Mulman navy. A grand battle ensued on the waters of the Moonsea and the Revenge was able to test the Gondsmen's secret projects. The Gondsmen had outfitted the Revenge with steel armor plating around the exterior of her hull, and a special steam engine to complement her sails. While the engine only worked briefly, it worked long enough to allow the Revenge to easily outmaneuver all five of the Mulman ships and destroy them in turn.

## Renekor

Renekor was a beholder from the Underdark that inadvertently wandered into, and eventually took control of, the demiplane known as Wisteria Vale.

Personality
Like other beholders, Renekor was exceedingly paranoid and saw anyone who entered into its lair as a threat. Renekor was definitely one to hold a grudge against anyone it believed had done it wrong.

Abilities
Once he made its home within, Renekor came to see Wisteria Vale as his own personal lair and everyone within as its personal playthings. Renekor floated around the village's manor house and dawdled in its ballroom, surrounded by constructs it believed it had created.

Relationships
While in the Underdark, Renekor commanded a small cadre of minions, though who exactly they were was unknown.

History
Sometime in the, Renekor went into slumber and dreamt itself into a new reality. It was transported to the demiplane of Wisteria Vale―modeled after the ruined village of the same name―which was actually created to house a powerful former-agent of the Harpers named Arrant Quill. Renekor however believed Wisteria Vale came into existence as a result of its dream state, a sanctuary that manifested for its own personal use.

Renekor's presence within the demiplane warped its otherwise peaceful, if not entirely genuine, environment. Normal sounds and smells either went missing or manifested differently than expected, objects in the demiplane changed hues to much more vivid colors, or lost all their color entirely, and incongruous weather patterns manifested at odd times.

Making itself home in the simulacrum of the idyllic village, Renekor took over control of the local manor house. The intruding beholder capture Arrant Quill and trapped him within a magic painting and created an illusory of the Harper that appeared real in nearly every regard.

Appendix
Appearances
Adventures
Candlekeep Mysteries: "The Curious Tale of Wisteria Vale"
References

---

## Reborn

The Reborn were servants of Mystra, usually Magisters, who were granted a new life for their service.

Rebirth
Those who underwent Mystra's Choice and became Reborn retained their prior intelligence, but were put in a new, whole and healthy body. They kept, however, a reverence for magic no matter what; it was possible for some to face Mystra's Choice again, sometimes for as little as praying in a consecrated altar to her.

Blind Reborn
Blind reborn were deprived of any memories of their prior lives, but had the power to cast a spell; this power was passed on to their offspring. However, casting this spell cost them some of their vitality.

Example spells they could cast included slightly modified invisibility, levitate, change self, blindness, strength, minor creation, locate object or obscure object, dispel magic, affect normal fires, dancing lights, feather fall, mending, light, shield, spider climb, darkness, ESP, wraithform, sleep, or flaming sphere.

Wise Reborn
Wise reborn kept their memories, but could only cast the spells they could use or were immune to as Magisters, once per day each. Never able to become magicians again, some found this to be a torment. They also gained the ability to, at will, turn themselves into wingless wonders, though they could not shift back at will; they only had the option to do this when touched by magic.

Known Reborn
Nouméa Drathchuld was an exception, neither blind nor wise, to the general rules for the Reborn.

Rumors & Legends
A few Reborn became Masters of Doors at Mystran temples. Others became spell component merchants, and patrons of adventurers, who sought old magic. Others became sages; per the Heralds, there were two that entered Candlekeep, and at least one retired to Highmoon in Deepingdale. A rare few became "sorcerer-slayers" (bounty hunters) or "magic hunters" (adventurers).

Appendix
References

## Refuge

A refuge was an enchantment which the Magister Maldiglas Turntower laid upon a number of areas in Faerûn. They responded to magic by thoroughly healing those within.

Use
Each refuge was most likely a spherical dome  across. They had no power to block entry, but any spell cast within, or enchanted object broken, or even breaking an object that was under the effects of a spell, triggered an effect upon the user. This effect could heal any hurts, purged feeblemind, geas, charm, fear, curses, and most other spell-borne alterations. They also provided warmth and coolness to the being inside, provided no other beings inside sought a different temperature. Being completely inside the refuge was not necessary to trigger its beneficial effects; simply intersecting its edge sufficed.

History
Maldiglas Turntower's project as the Magister was the creation of those refuges, which he began almost immediately upon gaining the mantle. He continued to create such refuges until his lifeforce drained completely.

After his death, a clay ball was broken at every magefair since, with a message inside each ball revealing the location of each such refuge. There was also a promise of retribution from Mystra if someone were to attempt to entrap, enclose, alter, or destroy such a refuge. Sources indicated the wrath of Mystra would also extend to those hurling dangerous spells into a refuge.

Locations
 Easting: There was a refuge in a little hollow flanked by duskwood trees at the north end of a knoll covered in shrubs, south of town, off a caravan road between two marshes that turned into shallow ponds in the wet season.
 Elmwood: Just west of the settled area, in the deep woods west of a pool known as Deepstar, there was a refuge between a felsul that split in three at ground level and the stump of a long-falled shadowtop.
 Eshpurta: A refuge lay west of a cluster of brambleberry bushes, itself west off the westernmost gate at the eastern end of the southernmost east-west ridges south of the settled area.
 Nashkel: South of the settled area there was a refuge between the Staring Ranger rock and the three black boulders known as the Eggs of the Dragon.
 Neverwinter: In Eldath's Patch.
 Saerb: Half a mile northwest by way of the Drunkard's Walk.
 Silverymoon: On the roof of the aviary due east of Mielikki's Glade.
 Soubar: East of Drunster's Well.
 Starmantle: In a triangular depression northwest of the settled area, in a little cleft on the shore leading to a  drop.
 Suzail: In the cleared center of a planting of juniper bushes parallel to Lake Azoun, northwest of Vangerdahast's Tower.
 Tilverton: Between two blue boulders, west of the Dragonbones.
 Trailstone: The southwestern end of a haunted burial mound southwest of the settled area held a refuge in its downslope.
 Waterdeep: In a cave within Mount Waterdeep, accessible through fourteen different gates spread across the city.

## Redcloth

Redcloths were red-hued pieces of fabric worn inside the undergarments by women of the Realms during their menstruation. They were used as a single layer or as a pair, and were sold by most inns and taverns during the day.

Usage
Redcloths were washed daily in scented water or cheap wine, and rinsed in clear water (such as collected rain water). There was no social stigma attached to this act, and finding washing facilities in civilized areas such as towns or cities was an easy task: this service was provided by every inn regardless of standing.

Women traveling the wilderness would wash redcloths in any sort of water, even using dew or stagnant swampwater. If no water could be found, old redcloths were buried to avoid being tracked by predators relying on scent.

Old redcloths that couldn't be used anymore were disposed of by tavern owners, usually becoming scrubbing rags.

Notes

## Reverberating crossbow of pain

Reverberating crossbow of pain was a powerful enchanted light crossbow from the land of Rashemen in the Unapproachable East.

Powers
This crossbow was a fierce weapon despite it being incredibly hard to handle. The weapon always violently vibrated, making it challenging to aim with for all but the most experienced marksmen. Reverberating crossbow of pain possessed a deadly +6 enchantment to its damage, and it dealt additional reverberating sonic damage to its targets. Additionally, the crossbow seemingly had an unlimited supply of magical booming missiles.

History
In the, reverberating crossbow of pain was lost in the Ashenwood forest of Rashemen. Its owner was found dead, their camp snowed in and frozen stiff. The tragic campsite was located not far from the massive evergreen tree, which was home of the Ashenwood's Wood Man spirit.

## Restore rune

Restore rune was a divine alteration and abjuration spell from the divination and guardian spheres that caused writings to appear.

Effects
When placed upon a large surface restore rune caused all normal and magical writings that had ever been placed upon the surface to appear clearly. If multiple writings appeared the caster selected one to read and study and the others vanished. The caster could choose to restore any triggers for revealed writings.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components a pinch of powdered gemstone, a piece of chalk, and three twigs from any plant bound together to form a triangle to cast.

History
Restore rune was granted to priests of Deneir, the Lord of All Glyphs.

## Reversion

Reversion was a divine alteration and invocation quest spell from the time sphere that reversed specific recent events.

Effects
The casting priest could only use this spell on allies within, undoing all physical harm the allies received during the last ten minutes provided the ally did not die from a single sudden attack. Allies that died from several attacks from cumulative damage during this time period were restored to life.

Any spells cast by allies during the last ten minutes were restored and could be cast again. Spell components used to cast these spells were also restored.

Casting the reversion spell was incredibly taxing on the caster, resulting in their aging one year each time the spell was cast.

Components
This spell did not require spell components of any kind to cast.

History
Reversion was a common spell used within the Church of Vhaeraun.

## Remove pain

Remove pain was a necromancy orison.

Effects
When cast, this spell helped the recipient deal with pain and provided minor healing. It had no effect on a healthy individual and could only be cast once a day on a targeted creature.

## Reindeer hooves

Reindeer hooves was a unique magical ability used by ice hunter shamans, who were specialty priests of Ulutiu, of the Great Glacier whose totem animal was Pindalpau-pau the Reindeer Mother.

Powers
Reindeer hooves bestowed a greatly enhanced movement rate for the ice hunter shaman. Additionally, the power granted them secure footing on any type of ice.

The ice hunter shaman could use this ability on themselves or convey it to another person. Reindeer hooves could be called upon once per day and lasted for up to one full hour.

The Remembrance Ritual was observed during Highharvestide. A somber event, the Remembrance Ritual was used as a day of self-reflection and to remember fallen comrades. The ritual concluded with a series of martial hymns thanking Anhur for any military victories that occurred during the past year.

## Resurrection Spring

Resurrection Spring was a spring in Northknife Pass across the Dustwall Mountains.

A small spring lay close to the highest point in the pass, surrounded by a flowerbed: it had tiny purple and white flowers, with occassional large crimson blossoms and pure white lillies. The water was so cold it numbed the mouth, and animals would not willingly drink it. It was also the last source of water in the pass, and thus known as Resurrection Spring.

## Revered mother

Revered mothers were specialty priestesses devoted to Hathor, the Dancer of Fortune within the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Activities
These holy women often delivered newborns, practically ensuring that zero life-threatening mishaps for the mother or child would occur during the process. Wealthy individuals were expected to pay for these services, but revered mothers aided those in need for free.

Culture
These individuals were always human women of Mulhorandi descent. They were "revered" because of their wisdom and knowledge.

Abilities
Revered mothers could used a number of powers on a daily basis including charm person or mammal, music of the spheres, divination, commune, consequence, charm monster, restore youth, and restoration. They could remove or dispel charms by touch as well. They could bestow a curse upon anyone threatening a pregnant woman or child.

Possessions
Revered mothers carried a variety of weapons including a sling, clubs, daggers, flails, and staves. Their preferred armor was padded, chainmail, leather armor, studded leather, and they sometimes carried a shield.

Description
This dark ceremony was performed by grave robbers serving Set and particularly infuriated followers of Osiris and Nephthys. Set's grave robbers completely defiled their target burial chamber by using it to create a dark altar to Set, looting the treasures found within, and animating the dead into undead.

## Reldra Wyntersar

Reldra Wyntersar was a socialite in Waterdeep in the 13 century DR. She was scorned by three noble lovers, whom she later attempted to murder.

History
Reldra was an up-and-coming young socialite. She was a beauty to look upon, and quite popular. Over time, she was taken as a lover by three noblemen, Neldror Artemel, Larondras Brokengulf, and Elglor Eagleshield. All three men promised her marriage and the wealth and prestige that would go with such a union. All three men reneged on their promise, and violently threw her out of their homes, telling the City Watch that she was a thief, which effectively cast her out of high society.

Decades later, in the summer of 1271 DR, Reldra managed to get hired as a servant at the Artemel Villa. All three of her former paramours were at the villa, and Reldra saw her chance at revenge. She slipped a paralytic agent into their drinks, then, after informing them of who she was and reminding them of what they'd done to her, she tipped their chairs over the edge of an upstairs gallery, killing Elglor and Neldror, and permanently maiming Larondras. She managed to kill several servants and guards, yelling "Marry me!" over and over again, before she was eventually slain.

Legacy
Waterdeep's nobility tried and failed to keep the incident a secret. It became the city's hottest topic of gossip for the following week.

Over two hundred years later, the phrase "Marry me!" was still uttered by Waterdhavian nobility as a warning of what could happen if they mistreated those of lesser station.

## Reeos

Reeos was a species of rare human-sized birds native to the Malatran Plateau of Kara-Tur.

Description
Reeos were large birds, the size of a medium humanoid creature. They had white plumage and green wings as well as green tail tips. Reeos had long beaks and rakish  long legs.

Personality
Reeos were not easily spooked and tended to be unmovable when guarding their eggs. If a menacing creature approached a nesting reeos, it menacingly rose on its legs and screamed in a head-splitting screech. The only way to remove a reeos from its nest was hurting or killing it. The birds tended to retreat if faced with strong foes and suffering some serious bodily harm.

Combat
In battle, reeos relied on distraction and disorientation caused by their incessant screeching and abundant feathers and down that filled the air during a struggle. The birds used their long beaks to pierce enemy armor and wing buffets that bludgeoned foes and released feathers disorienting the enemy. Lastly, reeos did not just emit your cacophony of screams but also could produce painful piercing shrieks that physically damaged the enemy up close. The more reeos produced such screeches at the same time, the more damage was done to the attacker.

Ecology
Reeos could be found nesting on river shores and by cascading waterfalls in the Living Jungle. The birds nested not closer than  apart. Hatchling reeos were tiny wrinkled creatures, bald and blind. If the parents were forced to leave their nests, reeos abandoned any chicks and unhatched eggs. Hatchlings were helpless and quietly chirped for their parents. Knowledgeable humanoids could raise a hatchling reeos if they manage to feed it once every hour.

Their eggs were green and white colored, weighted around  and were the size of a grapefruit. Reeos eggs were considered a valuable delicacy. Their value was great due to the delicious taste, reeos's rarity, and the fact that without a parent heating their eggs in a nest, reeos eggs rotted away within two days. It was impossible to successfully hatch a reeos egg without the parent bird's involvement. White reeos eggs possessed a magical quality to imbue the eater with intuitive knowledge of animal life.

History
In the late 14 century DR, the Year of Discovery on the Malatran calendar, a group of heroes of the Living Jungles embarked on a spiritual quest to help a dying saru village build a totem appeasing an angered and fickle earth spirit Ligela. The adventurers were sent toward a nearby waterfall to find special green stones needed for construction. There, the heroes found numerous species of nesting birds, including reeos. After disposing of the flying beasts, the adventurers found several of the stones in reeos's nests amid their now abandoned green eggs.

## Realm of the Mount

The Realm of the Mount was a region of volcanic, wood-cloaked wilderness located in the Border Kingdoms. It was a secretive realm, built upon murder and mistrust.

Description
To outsiders, the Realm of the Mount looked like unwelcoming, deserted wilderness. The rare homes were elevated log cabins, the only roads were muddy trails, and sparse sheeps and goats made for most of the cattle.

The main feature of the Realms was its "tombs", cairns hidden behind overgrowth which were rented by powerful individuals and used as a place to store stolen goods—or worse. It was usual for those cairns to be trapped and guarded by a diversity of creatures such as golems, crawling claws, dread warriors or skeletons.

Geography
This realm was centered on the Mount, a wood-covered volcano whose fume holes vented steaming vapors that sometimes caused individual trees to burn down in flames while letting the rest of the forest untouched. This led to the place being known as the flaming wood in ancient times. While the Mount wasn't prompt to erupt, earth tremors known as "grumble-thunder" were a common occurrence.

Government
Overdukes of the Mount
At the time of the Dread King Helemvo, the king had seven underlings known as "Overdukes"—who were regularly replaced whenever the king slaughtered one for treason or incompetence.

Harcounts
The Overdukes were served by about nine "Harcounts", who took care of actual work such as delivering messages, signing documents, or keeping records. Those Harcounts also kept watch on the Overdukes and reported anything suspicious to the king, who regularly "shuffled" Harcounts and Overdukes to prevent alliances from forming.

Bannerlords
The king had two bannerlords, who outranked the Harcounts and served as his personal messengers.

Hawklars
Hawklars were leaders among the dragonkin, and directed the patrols roaming the kingdom.

Trade
While most visitors were unwelcome in the Realm of the Mount, an exception was made for traveling merchants as long as they stayed on designated trails, markets and camping places. Dwarves were known to visit the realm to buy forging time in the volcano's secret rifts.

Defenses
The realm of the Mount was tightly guarded by patrols, usually comprised of three dragonkins, six gargoyles, two undead owlbears, and several pixies. In addition, they carried flying snakes in back-pouches to help in case of though fights. The realm was capable of rallying more than twenty such patrols in case of necessity.

Outsiders were particularly unwelcome in the Realm, with patrols savagely attacking them on sight. Visitors openly carrying magic items were attacked with extreme prejudice, and anyone trying to parley would get surrounded by at least three patrols until an Overduke decided what to do with them.

History
The region was originally a wilderness roamed by monsters and watched over by elves, who prevented gnomes and dwarves from settling in the volcano.

It was discovered by the wizard Xavander Relurvor during the, who was looking for a place to hide with his family after his brother Ormalin embezzled a large portion of Ravens Bluff's treasury. After settling in, he used the volcano to melt the treasure, minting counterfeit Amnian and Calishite coins with the resulting metals. Various family members faked their death and moved to the Mount, and after some internal strife Ormalin fell into a lava rift and Xavander established his kingdom.

Once his realm established, Xavander rented territory to Ravenaar individuals who could afford it. Those "Tombs" were officially sold as a place for their loved ones to rest, but were actually used as a hiding places where the "Tomb Holders" hid stolen goods, kidnapped rivals, or allies trying to lay low for a while.

When Xavander was killed by a more powerful wizard, his son Tholander took over. Not smart enough to actually rule, but smart enough to realize it, Tholander hired and ennobled some of the most vicious Ravenaars in exchange for them ruling in the shadows and acting as tutors for one of his own sons to prepare him to rule.

When Tholander died and Helemvo ascended to the throne, he assassinated his tutors and most of his siblings, cementing his position as the head of state. The "Dread King"  Helemvo then murdered several wealthy Tomb Holders, and used their wealth to build a small army of dragonkin, giving them gargoyles and undead creatures to command.

Notable Locations
Way of Screaming Swords
The ruins of Xavander's mansion could be found in Hool—if one managed to get past the various traps and magical guardians set up by the Dread King. Those ruins hid the Way of Screaming Swords, a magical portal linking the realm to the city of Ravens Bluff.

Inhabitants
The warm caves on the sides of the Mount sheltered many monsters and ne'er-do-wells. As such, fights were a common occurrence in the realm's forests.

## Renault Braveblood

Renault Braveblood was a human assassin adventurer active in the 14 century DR.

History
By the age of nineteen, Renault followed his mother's footsteps, who was a renowned assassin whose name was only known to the most wealthy houses of Faerûn. Like many young professionals, Renault faced the problem of not being able to get a job without experience and not being able to get experience without a job. With his mother's advice, Renault took on an adventuring career to learn to deliver death to his foes while surviving dungeon delves.

Sometime in the 14 century DR, Greenwood was among adventurers interested in being sent into a mysterious dungeon by an unnamed sorceress to retrieve the equally mysterious magical orb from its depths.

## Redwing (butterfly)

Redwings, also known as Morgan's redwings, were a species of butterfly found within the High Moor.

Description
As the name suggests, redwing butterflies had crimson wings with blood-red markings. They were considered among the most gentle and beautiful of the many species of butterflies found on the High Moor.

Ecology
Redwings were somewhat rare, although it was said that they had once been plentiful before being hunted almost to extinction by goblins for their wings, which they boiled to produce a red dye for their tribal art.

History
The name "Morgan's redwings" was in recognition of a druid named Morgan, who was active on the High Moor at some point prior to the mid-to-late 14 century DR. According to the tale, Morgan saved the species from extinction at the hands of goblins after the tribes discovered how to use them to produce red dye. The druid spread false tales that the redwing dye was poisonous, and that it was the real cause of a disease that had struck the goblin population. The lie worked, and the redwings were left alone to recover their numbers.

## Reig Redwaters

Reig Redwaters was a member of the Targos Guard, active in the early 14 century DR.

Description
Reig was a brown-haired human man who wore the Targos Guard's leather armor and was armed with a dagger and sword.

History
In the, the town of Targos in Icewind Dale was besieged by the Legion of the Chimera. One of the Legion's surprise attacks herded a troop of goblins by magical means through underground tunnels into the Targos docks, where Reig Redwaters and his partners, Brohn and Honest Jon, stood guard. Reig and Jon survived the attack, though Reig received a slash from a goblin axe, and Brohn was killed by an arrow to the neck.

Shortly after the attack began, the Wicked Wench docked, and a group of adventuring mercenaries from Luskan arrived to aid Targos. Reig greeted the adventurers, warning them of another band of goblins nearby, and asked them to find the harbor master Magdar Shenlen‎‎ and acquire some newly-arrived healing draughts for his wound. Reig Redwaters remained guarding the docks against goblin arsonists, as its safety was crucial to the town's survival.

## Red Wizard blade

A Red Wizard blade was a potent type of enchanted dagger used by the Red Wizards of Thay as of the late 15 century DR. They were notorious, owing to the fact that no cleric could save an individual slain with one.

Description
These daggers were made of steel. They had a narrow blade and a grim design.

Powers
Red Wizard blades drew power from the Negative Energy plane to unleash harmful necromantic energies upon any being struck by it. The veins of those injured by a Red Wizard blade became visible and black beneath the skin. Such injuries killed the victim readily, and they could not be raised or resurrected by any normal means short of divine intervention or the use of a tablet of reawakening.

History
The blades were designed and crafted by the Red Wizards using processes known only to them. In response to their use of such potent necromancy, the Witches of Rashemen—longtime enemies of the Red Wizards—devised the tablet of reawakening.

By the late 15 century DR, some Thayan agents in the Sword Coast region were known to carry these blades. Following the capture of one of their own by the Harpers in the mid-1480s DR, Red Wizards tracked one of the agents, Edgin Darvis, to his home in Targos and used the dagger to slay his wife, Zia. Eleven years after this event, in the late 1490s DR, Edgin's longtime friend Holga Kilgore was also slain with a Red Wizard blade wielded by the Red Wizard Sofina. Fortunately, Edgin was able to use a tablet of reawakening to revive her.

Notable Owners
 Sofina, a Red Wizard active on the Sword Coast on behalf of Szass Tam as of the late 15 century DR.

## Realm of Wailing Fog

The Realm of Wailing Fog was a mysterious land found on the eastern edge of the Hullack Forest and west of the Thunder Peaks mountain range in Cormyr.

Description
The realm was a collection of permanently mist-covered secluded fens with eerie calls echoing through it. By the 14 century DR, the misty wetland hid long-ruined towers. Everyone who traversed the Hullack Forest or passed along the East Way that curved south towards the nearby Realm of Wailing Fog, testified of a strange and sinister feeling of being watched.

History
During the century that followed the Spellplague, the magical chaos did not affect the Realm of Wailing Fog. The land remade a forbidding mystery. None dared enter the Realm, not even the Eldreth Veluuthra. In the 15 century DR, there were unsubstantiated rumors that claimed that the Realm of Wailing Fog was home to a coven of hags.

## Renfeld

Renfeld was a member of the Harpers who clandestinely operated in the City of Coin, Athkatla in the late 14 century DR.

History
In the, Renfeld was ambushed on the streets of Athkatla. His assailants injected him with fast-acting poison; luckily, a group of adventurers led by Abdel Adrian walked in on the attack. However, they were too late to stop the poisoning. Renfeld was unable to move and already had attempted to unsuccessfully neutralize poison. The man asked the adventurers to bring his dying body to a large building in Athkatla's Wave District, the Galvarey Estate, secretly a Harper safehouse. His Harper allies could cure the affliction, but Renfeld needed to be taken there post haste. It was unknown if Abdel Adrian managed to deliver the dying man to the Harper agent Rylock in time to save his fleeting life.

## Rechel

Rechel was a tiefling of Elturel who was among those kicked out of the city in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Rechel had dark-red skin, black hair, and two large horns that curved backwards like those of a ram. She dressed in simple clothes, a tunic and trousers.

History
Like other Elturian tieflings, Rechel was outcast from the city following the Descent in the . When the people of Elturel witnessed the horrors of the Nine Hells firshard, it was decided that any "devilkin" would be forced out of the holy capital. Many of the tieflings fell under the leadership of Zevlor, who led them west along the Risen Road towards Baldur's Gate. Unfortunately, goblin raids forced the refugees to seek shelter at the Emerald Grove, inadvertently endangering the druid's within their own home.

As tension grew between the druids and their tiefling guests, Rechel become more and more frustrated. She was among those that felt the selfishness and callousness of head druid Kagha would be the death of her kind.

## Reflection shield

The reflection shield was a unique and aptly-named small enchanted shield said to originated from the Myth Rhynn by some accords.

Powers
This shield possessed a +1 defensive enchantment. However, its true power was in it reflecting certain types of damage back at the attacker. Virtually all thrown weapons, darts, buttets, arrows, and bolts of mundane and magical nature were reflected back when hitting its surface. Additionally, such spells as flame arrow and magical stone were sent back to their source. Traps like snares and spikes also left the shield's wielder unaffected. This shield also protected the wielder from several types of monster missile-like and spell-like abilities.

History
The reflection shield's history was obscured by time. It was used as a wall-mounted decoration from an old festhall. From there, it was abandoned in a shop's backroom until it was acquitted to be used as ceremonial armor by a young noble sometime before the . The shield's magics became apparent when an attempt on the nobleman's life ended in spells being reflected back at the caster. When sages researched this reflection shield, they managed to find connections to the ancient elven city of Myth Rhynn, or possibly to a similarly old site.

In 1369 DR, the reflection shield was sold at Ribald Barterman's Adventurer Mart in Athkatla's Waukeen's Promenade.

Appearances

## Redspring

Redspring was a small village in the Stonelands of northern Cormyr.

Description
In the late 1360s DR Redspring was a booming frontier town, consisting of newly-built and half-built structures inhabited by optimistic merchants and traders sponsored by the Crown. A good deal of these businesspeople came with retinues of bodyguards, expecting trouble on the venture. The roads were just dirt paths, and combined with the influx of traffic to transform into a muddy morass with no indication of improvement. In its new and unfinished state the village had no inns or taverns, and construction supplies were scarce, with people digging up their own building stone nearby.

Geography
Redspring was located in the Stonelands, south of the Storm Horns and north of Arabel. Slingdyke lay to the east, the Forgotten Keep to the west, and Barrenstone a shorter distance to the south.

Geographical Features
The soil around Redspring was rich in iron, staining the springs a rusty red and giving it the taste of old blood. The springs there never froze even in the coldest winters, and despite the taste were one of the most reliable places to find fresh, pure water in the area. The springs flowed into the Sunset Stream, which later joined the Starwater River close to the source.

Defenses
As of 1367 DR the village was defended by a Purple Dragons detachment in a tiny keep, guarding work crews who raised earthen ramparts in a wide ring around the town. Four War Wizards were also deployed to the site, housed in a newly-built mansion. Their presence was a great encouragement to the settlers, who arrived in ever-greater numbers thanks to the increased security. The village also received regular patrols of soldiers from Arabel

Despite the presence of Cormyr's defenders, Redspring was regularly spied upon by Zhentarim agents riding feywings. The village was considered to be in a risky state thanks to its remote location and dangerous surroundings, with the expectation of either great fortune or disaster.

History
The site had been a meeting place for shepherds in the region, though it went through long periods of abandonment when the wilderness of the Stonelands became too dangerous to bear. In the 1360s DR the site was chosen by the Crown for building as part of a wave of expansion on Cormyr's borders, and it became inundated with settlers and other opportunists taking advantage of the Crown's grant money.

## Redwood

Redwoods were a species of trees found in several locations across the Realms, known for their sturdiness and massive size.

Habitats
 Redwoods grew in the High Forest and were used by elvish craftsmen to carve impressive furniture out of, including thrones.
 The Forest of Wyrms in the Western Heartlands had a population of majestic massive redwood trees that reached several hundreds feet tall and to feet wide.
 Cormyr's Helmlands had the Redwoods forest that perished during the Spellplague, but in the century that followed a new generation of redwood trees grew to maturity in place of their perished progenitors.
 Hartsvale, the land of giants and giant trees, exported quality redwood that was used to fashion fashion masts and keels by shipbuilders of Westgate.

Usages
 Orcs and tanarukka that served Daggerford's Devil Gorgomorz in the, were supplied with orcish-made redwood shields.  each, they lacked in appearance but were very effective nevertheless.
 Redwood was used in construction of doors in Tethyr. Redwood doors were in use even in the royal palace of Tethyr in the 15 century DR.
 Redwood furniture was in use in the land of Zakhara. A huge table of carved redwood could be found in the Ivory Tower of Dar al-Bayda in the 14 century DR.

## Redspawn arcaniss

Redspawn arcanisses were a species of dragonspawn that wielded potent and destructive fire magics.

Description
Redspawn arcanisses were largely humanoid in form, and stood some  tall and weighed  as adults. They were covered in red scales with flame-like yellow and oranges markings. These markings were lighter and more extensive on hatchlings, but darkened to red as they matured, eventually becoming a near-black red shade.

Personality
Redspawn arcanisses reveled in pain and destruction. They were fanatically devoted to Tiamat and believed that every destructive act they did should honor her.

Abilities
Like many other dragonblood creatures, redspawn arcanisses possessed both low-light vision and darkvision. They could not be paralyzed or forced asleep by magic, and like other redspawn, they were unharmed by fire and high heat. However, they were vulnerable to low temperatures.

Redspawn arcanisses were natural sorcerers with a strong predilection for fire spells. They could cast acid splash, detect magic, ghost sound, message, ray of frost, and touch of fatigue each six times per day; burning hands, magic missile, chill touch, and true strike each seven times per day; Melf's acid arrow and scorching ray each six times per day, and fireball four times per day. Additionally, their affinity for fire magic meant that each time they cast a fire spell, the energy rejuvenated them slightly.

Combat
Redspawn arcanisses enjoyed combat and had a tendency to charge directly into melee, using fire spells recklessly and relying on their affinity to stay alive. If their fiery magic proved ineffective, they'd retreat, only to return later with other dragonspawn in search of revenge. Unlike many mages, redspawn arcanisses were capable of wearing light armor and carrying a light shield without risk of it interfering with their sorcery, though they always kept one hand free for spellcasting. They often carried a simple weapon, typically a light mace, in case melee became necessary.

If arcanisses spotted a servant of or creature associated with good dragons, they prioritized killing that foe before all others, even in the face of dire opposition.

Society

Redspawn arcanisses were created by Tiamat as combat spellcasters, and like all of Tiamat's dragonspawn, they were fanatically religious. They believed that they were her favored spawn, that they'd been mandated to torch the land and cleanse it of all non-dragonkind, and that they would rule over the rest of dragonkind after this cleansing was accomplished. They were guided in these matters by their holy book, the Scrolls of Fire, which they believed had been written by Tiamat herself.

On a day to day basis, however, arcanisses seemed far less than such a claimed destiny would suggest. They lived in small, nomadic bands of two to six individuals, never staying in one place for too long, nor gathering in large groups too often, to avoid growing dependent on others. When two groups of arcanisses met, they mingled freely and shared news, before splitting into new groups. Occasionally, multiple bands would work together to defeat a common foe but more often the most numerous band would take up that task and send the others away.

Arcanisses lived rough, and because of their nomadic ways, they rarely made any lasting impact on a given area, though they sometimes caused forest fires as they traveled. They were known to train to further their abilities as sorcerers.

Ecology
Redspawn arcanisses were carnivorous creatures who preferred their meat fresh and well-roasted. They typically lived in warm hills, making temporary lairs in large caves. They had a strong hoarding instinct, but did not take more treasure than they could carry. As arcanisses did not make items, nearly all of their hoard and equipment was stolen, or traded for from other, more settled dragonspawn. They preferred finer-quality items, especially ones with a draconic theme, and favored jewelry and art objects for their hoard.

Female arcanisses were fertile for one week each year, and laid only a single egg one month after mating. Females preferred to lay their eggs in naturally warm sites such as hot springs, volcanic vents, or even just a well-tended fire pit. Eggs hatched approximately after two weeks of incubating; hatchling arcanisses were adult-sized and capable of speech and spellcasting, but brighter in color. The adults of their band gave the hatchlings a week, maybe two, of instruction both religious and practical. After that, the hatchlings and adults split into new groups and left.

Redspawn arcanisses, along with redspawn firebelchers, were rumored to live in the Greypeak Mountains.

## Resino

Resino was a young man from Beregost who became the reason for the creation of rings of clear thought some years before the late 14 century DR.

History
Resino was just a young man when he became afflicted with voices that incessantly attacked his mind. Resino turned to a local temple for help in ending his miserable existence. The temple's clergy was unable to determine what kind of demon was plaguing Resino. Yet, the church refused to take Resino's life, instead, it ceased rings of clear thought that would help with Resino's strange affliction. However, these rings enhanced the young man's senses but did nothing to stop the voices. Eventually, unable to suffer anymore, Resino took his own life.

## Refuse pit (Port Nyanzaru)

The refuse pit of Port Nyanzaru, Chult was the city's garbage dump.

Description
The pit was a huge and greasy sinkhole that dominated the outskirts of the Old City outside Port Nyanzaru's western walls. The smell was intense, and even a breezy day offered no relief from the stomach-churning stench.

The pit was was over  deep and filled with a sea of festering trash, rotting flesh and bones, and rancid water. This vast amount of waste filled up to  of the deep pit, and while this level could rise, it never overflowed thanks to an unknown number of otyughs living and feasting deep beneath the surface of the trash. These monsters could be heard rusting through the garbage on quiet nights, but they rarely posed a threat to anyone who didn't enter the pit. Less docile were the undead that prowled the dump, including ghouls and the occasional ghast, wraith, flameskull, and spawn of Kyuss. Naturally, the dump was also home to countless vermin, including insects, rats, and ravens.

A passage leading to catacombs beneath the Old City's ziggurats was located in the wall of the refuse pit beneath the surface of the trash.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, the refuse pit had gained an unfortunate reputation as a place where corpses were dumped, providing a steady food supply for the undead. The albino dwarf Favin Kreeger was paid by the city's criminals to hide murder victims in the dump.

Following the end of the death curse, a wight necromancer named Jaru Tasca acquired a piece of the Cyst, a powerful necromantic mass of flesh, and plotted to use it to transform Port Nyanzaru's residents into undead. Her minions would rise from the refuse pit to capture victims and return them to her secret base in the catacombs beneath the Old City. The number of undead in the pit soon grew and began to threaten the city.

## Resin

Resin was a broad class of substances produced from the sap of plants and some animals, and used for a wide array of purposes. Depending on the type, it could also be known as gum.

Availability
Aurora's Emporium sold a variety of high-quality gums and resins at reasonable prices, suited for the rituals and practices of priests, the arcane experimentation of wizards, as well as more common variants suited for everyday use.

Usages
Resins were widely used in the production of food, perfume, candy, adhesives, and alchemical recipes. They were also used in medicine for their curative properties. Certain resins could also be used in the construction of furniture and buildings as glue and as varnish.

Varieties

Resins were produced by a wide variety of plants. More valuable examples included:
 Asafetida (gum asafetida)
 Benzoin (gum benzoin)
 Camphor (gum camphor), from the camphor tree.
 Cassia, from the cassia tree.
 Dragons' blood, from the dragons' blood tree.
 Elemi
 Galbanum
 Gum arabic, from the acacia tree.
 Frankincense, from the frankincense tree.
 Lacquer, from the lacquer tree.
 Myrrh, from the myrrh tree.
 Onycha
 Talsh, from nychaki.
 Storax

A few varieties of valuable resins came from animals, including:
 Ambergris, from whales.
 Lakh resin, from the lakh insect.
 Propolis, from bee honeycomb.

Resins could also be changed into substances far-removed from their original forms, such as:
 Amber, a solidified golden-orange resin valued as a gem.
 Pitch, a refined resin used for construction.
 Terebinth, a distilled resin used in alchemy.
 Ostorian resin, a resin of unidentified ancient design.

Appendix

External Links

References

---

## Red Bazaar

The Red Bazaar was a major marketplace located in the Market Ward of Port Nyanzaru, Chult. It was where the city's residents shopped for their daily needs.

Location
The marketplace consisted of broad rows of colorful stands that dominated the Market Ward east of the Grand Coliseum and south of the Hall of Gold. Nearby the market were two of the most notable inns in the city: the Thundering Lizard and Kaya's House of Repose.

Services

The Red Bazaar was largely a marketplace of locals buying and selling necessities, trade goods, and household products to other locals. This included trade in clothing, insect repellent (both in the form of incense and salves), rain catchers, tej, alchemist's supplies, and food (such as local fruits, vegetables, and meats, including dinosaur meat).

Merchants seeking to sell their wares, as well as customers seeking special orders, were directed to the Master of the Market. As of the late 15 century DR, this position was held by Terrah Initoo.

History
The origins of the name of the Red Bazaar were lost to history. The people's best guess was that it was named for the blood that stained the marketplace's gutters, which was a product of the huge pieces of dinosaur meat processed there.

As of the late 15 century DR, the Red Bazaar was one of the three most important marketplaces within Port Nyanzaru, the other two being the Grand Souk and the Jewel Market. The eclectic mix of products for sale meant that several of the city's merchant princes had a stake in the business of the bazaar, most notably including Kwayothé and Ekene-Afa.

During the events of the death curse, one of the Bazaar's merchants was Giregi Igiri, who specialized in alchemical ingredients and products.

## Revenge of the Cult

Revenge of the Cult is a one-round tournament Planar Adventure module designed by Michael White and set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in 2000, and intended for adventurers high levels.

Summary
In 1373 DR, a mighty dracolich was sent by the Cult of the Dragon to aid Myrkyssa Jelan in her war with the Vast. However, through a heroic sacrifice, the undead wyrm was bound to a valley east of Ravens Bluff, near the village of Grey Hills. In 1373 DR, two planar powers, gith named Ilkaren Tysor who served the Silver Mask, and planetar Lithuriel who served Garthiel, became aware of planar magics being worked in the valley. The Cult of the Dragon and the Church of Tiamat allied for some nefarious reason under the leadership of mage Bediss, who entered a pact with a powerful fiend Verigrim. The pact's payment was opening a gate into the Elemental Plane of Water. A group of heroes was sent to the eastern town to investigate the strange planar magic. Along the way, the adventurers looked into many disappearances of Grey Hills's children, who were massacred by the sadistic dracolich. The heroes faced mercenaries and forces of the Cult and, possibly, slew the undead wyrm. In the end, the intel on the Cult was delivered to the Silver Mask and to the city of Ravens Bluff's officials.

## Rendilar Bluthlock

Rendilar Bluthlock was a member of the criminal underworld of Scornubel in the mid-14 century DR.

Possessions
He wore boots.

History
In late, Tornar the Eye carried a message to Rendilar Bluthlock that he could spend freely, backed by Belgon Bradaskor, to send as many people to their deaths as he wished in hunting down Shandril Shessair. He led them in the hunt for spellfire. He was slain before the caravan arrived at Triel.

Rumors
The men under his command were under the impression that one of the caravan's people was a mage from the Cult of the Dragon who was also a double agent for the Arcane Brotherhood.

## Regrar

Regrar was a Purple Dragon Knight in Azoun IV's personal retinue during the mid–14 century DR.

Relationships
In Azoun's personal retinue, he served with others including Daervin and Forold.

History
Regrar was leading the effort to dig a grave for Daervin in a gorge near Eveningstar during King Azoun IV's visit to Tessaril Winter, after Highknight Daervin was slain by a malaugrym. While digging, he extensively discussed the subject of Tessaril's exact relationship to the king, explaining to his fellow Highknights that Lord Tessaril was close personally with Azoun, and that he was certain of her loyalty. He later spotted that he was being scried on by Hlael Toraunt and Korthauvar Hammantle, and warned his fellow knights to fetch a War Wizard.

## Reglin

Reglin was a proxy of the Norse power known as Heimdall.

Description
Reglin was a tall and strong man, with blond hair and brown eyes.

Abilities
Reglin could invoke Heimdall to cast prismatic wall.

Possessions
Reglin wielded a broadsword for a weapon. He typically favored dressing in clothes and decorations that were shades of red and yellow.

Activities
As a proxy of a god of guards and sentries, Reglin himself lived as a devoted sentry of the Aesir, spending much of his time guarding the Bifrost. When he wasn't on duty at the Bifrost he spent his time at Gloamingard, a longhouse he built to rest at when not on duty.

History
In the, a group of adventurers from the city of Ravens Bluff journeyed to the plane of Ysgard in the Great Wheel cosmology in search of the legendary Well of Mimir, having been instructed by the city's Ministry of Art to do so in order to learn how a powerful githyanki artifact known only as the Great Machine could be destroyed. They arrived in the layer of Asgard by means of a ship, either the Fyrfload or Laughing Gull, having ridden as passengers from the gate-town of Glorium through the Whirling Gate. While coasting through the waters of Asgard, they eventually came across Reglin's Gloamingard along the shoreline.

Prior to their arrival, the wandering realm of the Seelie Court had arrived to the area and asked for Reglin's hospitality. He was quite surprised, but obliged, not wishing to offend Queen Titania. When the adventurers entered Reglin's hall they found him socializing with Titania, whilst his courtiers were busy trying to keep her rowdy fey under control. He was behaving like a nervous courtier, hoping that his talk would bore her so much that she would want to leave.

The adventurers were greeted by Eomar, an Asgardian warrior and member of the Fated, who went on to introduce them to Reglin and Titania. The Queen welcomed them to the plane on behalf of the Aesir, the Norse pantheon, leaving a shocked Reglin to choke from the statement. She then invited them to partake in the grand feast that Reglin had readied and expressed hope they would enjoy later festivities, which left Reglin concerned since he hadn't prepared anything.

After being in Gloamingard for some time, growing irritated from the fey, the adventurers watched as Queen Titania gathered the fey around her with a clap and expressed a desire for entertainment in the form of a series of contests between the mortals and fey. Unwilling to jeopardize the lives of his own men, who were largely befuddled by fey glamour and thus unlikely to perform well, he turned to the adventurers and pulled them aside. He apologized to them for not having formally extended his hospitality earlier and beseech them to help him, offering each a silver armring worth 20 gold pieces, plus a gold armring for each contest they participated in.

As the competition was coming to a close Gloamingard's copy of the Gjallarhorn sounded, warning of an incoming attack. Reglin jumped to his feet, brandishing his sword and shouting, "Foes come, let friends gather!". The eighty petitioners in the hall, who had been dancing and singing with the fey, transformed into einheriar. Reglin rallied everyone else around him, arming them and barring the front door after people came in and identified the attackers as a raiding party of frost giants from Jotunheim. Whether he survived the battle or not was unknown.
