## Reithwin

Reithwin was a ruined and abandoned town located between Baldur's Gate and Elturel in the Shadow-cursed Lands.

Description
Once a haven for worshippers of Selûne, the town along with the surrounding lands was consumed by the Shadow Curse unleashed by Ketheric Thorm, a former devoted Selûnite follower who eventually turned to the cult of the Absolute as leading general.

Geography
The town was located on the banks of the River Chionthar to the west of Elturel in the Shadow-cursed Lands. This town could be traveled to from a mountain pass known as the Risen Road or through the Underdark (via a temple of Selûne). A toll was required for passage through the town. The Grand Mausoleum was constructed to serve as the final resting place of the Thorm family.

History

Reithwin was founded by the followers of Selûne.

According to the Infernal Mason from the House of Hope, Reithwin and the Moonrise Towers used to be a happy home to his then master Ketheric Thorm until a tragedy happened and his wife Melodia passed away.

Circa the, Ketheric had turned to the goddess of Shar to cope with the loss of his daughter Isobel. Forming a Sharran army of Dark Justiciars from the Underdark, the town of Reithwin became a battlefield between Ketheric's followers and opposing forces of Shar. As Ketheric was beginning to lose the war against the anti-Sharran forces he unleashed the Shadow Curse out of desperation, turning the whole town into a darkened wasteland.

In the year 1492 DR, a century after the Shadow Curse devoured the town, the curse was still lingering, and anyone daring to pass through would require carrying a moonlantern or being Sharran-blessed to survive.

If the shadow curse were to be lifted by the Absolute Adversaries, people from the Chionthar Valley and Baldur's Gate were ready to resettle the ruined town mere six months after the end of the Absolute crisis.

Notable Locations

Service Shops & Temples
 Sharran Sanctuary, an underground sanctuary in the center of the town.
 Reithwin Tollhouse, local tollhouse controlled by Gerringothe Thorm.
 Mason's Guild, a guildhall established by Morfred.
Inns & Meeting Halls
 The Waning Moon, Thisobald Thorm's distillery and tavern.
Landmarks
 Grand Mausoleum, the Thorm family's mausoleum, which concealed a secret passage to the Gauntlet of Shar.
 Graveyard
 House of Healing, a medical institution controlled by Malus Thorm.

Notable Inhabitants
 Gerringothe Thorm
 Malus Thorm, the Reithwin House of Healing's Head Surgeon
 Sister Anna Lidwin, a Darkcloak and House of Healing staff member
 Morfred, the leader of the Selûnite resistance
 Halfred, a member of the Selûnite resistance
 Jerald, a scout
 Harold, a scout
 Thisobald Thorm
 Madeline, bartender in the Waning Moon
 Marc Jacobs, the Waning Moon patron
 Benjamin Blanchet, the Waning Moon patron
 Netasha, a Dark Justiciar who lived in the town

## Reyni Delapond

Commander Reyni Delapond was an Amnian Council Army officer who commanded Citadel Rashturl in the 14 century DR.

Personality
Reyni had an infectiously tenacious and positive attitude that made him quite popular with the other soldiers who gave him their trust and loyalty.

Abilities
Reyni was an exceptionally strong fighter, very quick on his feet, and possessed of incredible charisma.

History
Reyni was a common soldier who worked his way up through the ranks. He won the command of Citadel Rashturl over the competition such as Arbas Rosznar, but came to fame during the First Dragonspear War of 1356 DR when he fought many baatezu and ogres. His command of Citadel Rashturl was effective and well-executed, including a training regimen he devised that educated his troops in tactics, strategy, and history. Thanks to his leadership and administration, he made his garrison the most elite and well-trained group in Amn, and the posting in the isolated, spartan Citadel was considered a "pearl" that any soldier would be happy to get. His command continued through 1370 DR, maintaining the very high standards he had built.

## Reynald

Reynald was a duck-breeder from the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
Reynald was a beloved family man, a father and a husband, who perished in the, shortly before the armies of the Cult of the Absolute attacked the city of Baldur's Gate. Reynald's tombstone was commissioned to be carved by Candulhallow's Tombstones in the Lower City. The freshly-carved grave marker was stored in Candulhallow's workshop. It read: "Beloved Reynald. Father, husband, duck-breeder. Gone too soon."

## Returning pike

Returning pikes were enchanted throwing polearms, found in the Western Heartlands of Faerûn in the late 15 century DR.

Powers
Returning pikes were imbued with the returning dwarvish enchantment of Clan Ticklebeard that pulled the pike out and ruched it back to the thrower's hands. Additionally, they possessed a +1 enchantment that guided the weapon toward its target and increased its damage, making it magical.

History
In the, returning pikes were known for the Crazy Wilbur battle technique invented by the Ticklebeards. Pikes and other returning weapons were swallowed by the enemies and then called back, making a gory mess in the process. One such magic pike was in the hands of Grat the Trader, a goblin of the Cult of the Absolute. The weapon itself was looted from nearby locales targeted by the cult in 1492 DR.

## Reaper's Embrace

The Reaper's Embrace was a suit of magical plate mail armor associated with the Myrkulyte faith. It was famously worn by General Ketheric Thorm during the Absolute crisis of the late 15 century DR.

Description
The armor was quite beautifully crafted, with adornments that evoked both skeletal forms and symbology of Myrkul himself. The breastplate was crafted to resemble the bones of a ribcage, featuring an open maw gorget with thorn-like teeth that threatened to swallow the wearer whole. The pauldrons of the Reaper's Embrace were decorated with detached, decrepit hands grasping onto golden skulls. Likenesses of other skulls, skeletons, and numerous iterations of Myrkul's holy symbol were engraved across several pieces of the armor.

The center of the armor's breastplate held a golden adornment, within which Ketheric Thorm set his netherstone.

Ketheric preferred to wear a chain shirt and elaborately embroidered gambeson were worn underneath the armor.

Powers

The wearer of the armor could incite the reaper's rigidity, a supernatural state that prevented from being moved or knocked over against their will. Unfortunately, this power also prevented the wearer from moving with any real grace or agility.

One or more times per day the wearer of Reaper's Embrace could let out a howl of the dead. This inflicted numbness in the extremities of any creatures within a  radius.

History
Ketheric remained in possession of the armor as of the, following his second conversion, fromt the Sharran to the Myrkulyte faith.

## Reagan Splint

Reagan Splint was an inhabitants of the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Reagan was a large and muscular half-orcish woman. She had gray skin and fur and styles her hair into a red mohawk.

History
In the, an army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees in the process along the way and provoking fear and paranoia inside the city walls. Reagan was outraged by the number of public executions that took place on the plaza outside of the Basilisk Gate. She wrote to the Baldur's Mouth Gazette about the issue every tenday and every tenday, the broadsheet ignored the half-orc's calls. During that time, Reagan spent some time renting lodgings at Nortale's Hostel until an incident that had her removed from the reestablishment.

## Republic of Tivaan

The Republic of Tivaan was a merfolk republic on the Hmur Plateau, beneath the Sea of Fallen Stars, that formed out of rebellion against the oppressive king of Thalorlyn.

History
In, in the midst of the Third Serôs War, a group of spies discovered that Coronal Essyl Merynth of the Aryselmalyr Empire and King Kyron of Thalorlyn were secretly allied with a trio of fiendish krakens for further power. This revelation led to the formation of the Republic of Tivaan. Despite the power of their kraken allies and the artifact known as the Emerald Eye, the rebels managed to depose Kyron and his loyalists a year later. They were all banished from the Hmur Plateau, disestablishing Thalorlyn and having the Republic of Tivaan take its place.

Having lost his puppet state, the focus of the Third Serôs War shifted as Coronal Essyl began sending his forces against the upstart republic. In, the republic would forge an alliance against Aryselmalyr with the Arcana of Humbar, a usually isolationist nation of morkoths.

Deposed from the Thalorlyn throne, but still an ally to the Coronal, the exiled King Kyron sought to create a new center of power for himself. He and his loyalists constructed the temple of Vynagyr along the slopes of the Nalaan Plateau, finishing it in, and dedicated it to Those Who Sleep Below. From his new base of power Kyron worked together with Essyl to forge alliances with various evil creatures of the Inner Sea, including morkoths and scrags, hoping the addition of such forces could help them to quell the rebellions against Aryselmalyr.In the original text the temple is referred to as being dedicated to "long forgotten evil powers". With Dagon retconned in Grand History of the Realms as a member of Those Who Sleep Below and as the reason behind Kyron's madness, it retroactively suggests that the temple is dedicated to them.

In, the first Passing of the shalarin occurred, as the Wildtide Portal and shalarin flooded into the Inner Sea from the Sea of Corynactis. The newcomers were quickly attacked by the forces of Essyl and Kyron, with the added aid of their Emerald Eye. The Republic of Tivaan, the Dukars of Myth Nantar, and the Arcana of Humbar would quickly rush to the aid of the shalarins and summarily forge an alliance with them against Aryselmalyr.

In, the Dukars would finally slay the mad exiled king and take his Emerald Eye into custody. His loyalists then divided, with some joining the Republic of Tivaan and others maintaining their allegiance to Coronal Essyl. The Third Serôs War would eventually come to an end in . From then on the Republic maintained peaceful relations with the other nations of Serôs.

Years later in, the Fourth Serôs War began as a massive horde of merrows and koalinths invaded the Hmur Plateau. Their numbers too great to overcome, they overran the Plateau and brought about the fall of the Republic of Tivaan. Feeling desperation, a mermaid stole the Emerald Eye from one of the towers of the Dukars, believing the powerful artifact could drive away the invaders. However, she became corrupted by the Emerald Eye, taken over by what some believed to be the spirit of King Kyron contained within the artifact.

With her mind corrupted by the Emerald Eye, the mermaid imposed herself upon the invaders, declaring them to be the Second Kingdom of Kyron and becoming their leader in a continued conquest of the Hmur Plateau. The war would eventually come to an end in, with the possessed mermaid killed and the Emerald Eye taken back into the custody of the Dukars. The mermaid's body would be buried in the Plains of Duna, subsequently renamed the Haunted Plains, and the Kingdom of Thalvan would be founded as the successor to the Republic of Tivaan by King Darmox.It is never stated within either of the primary sources, Sea of Fallen Stars and Grand History of the Realms, what the "Second Kingdom of Kyron" was. In its summary of the Serôs Wars on page 153, Sea of Fallen Stars states that the Fourth Serôs War's belligerents were the "Second Kingdom of Kyron vs. koalinth and merrow hordes." This would seem to imply that the Republic of Tivaan was this "Second Kingdom of Kyron", however, it seems unlikely that the republic who rebelled against Kyron's rule would take such a name for themselves and is contradicted by the text on the two cited pages stating that the destruction of the Second Kingdom of Kyron adversely affected the Serôsian population of koalinths — if koalinths were in opposition to this Second Kingdom, then its destruction would logically be advantageous to them. This leaves the conclusion that the "Second Kingdom of Kyron" must refer to the collective forces under the mermaid corrupted by the Emerald Eye and the spirit of King Kyron within it.

## Red Wolf

The Red Wolf, also known as the Lone Hunter, was a being said to be the mightiest and fiercest warrior among the wild elf tribes of Elfharrow. Some claimed he was merely a lone killer, while others characterized him as a righter of wrongs.

Description
Little was known about the Red Wolf save for tales told by the tribes. He was said to have the appearance of a tall elf dressed as a hunter, except when he instead appeared as a massive, red-furred wolf.

Activities
As of the late 15 century DR, he was said to hunt alone across the blasted lands of Elfharrow. On occasion, young warriors from among the Elfharrow tribes would seek him out to prove themselves his equal in combat, but all who did so inevitably died by his hand.

In the rare event that the Red Wolf chose to meet with a tribe, the chiefs and shamans of Elfharrow always listened to his words and obeyed his instructions.

## Red Death

Red Death was a unique marble bastard sword that housed the soul of Sir Ian Glae James Graham, a paladin of Tyr, created in the 14 century DR.

Description
Red Death was an intimidating weapon that was formed from crimson marble by using a stone shape spell. Its sharp edge was polished and strengthened with silver, and its hilt was swathed in black dragon hide.

Powers
The sword bore a powerful +4 enchantment that left devastating damage in its wake and guided the wielder's arms to hit the targets more easily. With a paladin's soul woven into the weapon, it allowed the wielder to cast telepathy, read languages, detect magic, strength, heal, and detect evil once per day. When Red Death was wielded against clerics of evil deities, Sir Graham granted the wielder a special ability to cast disintegrate trice per day.

History
In the early-to-mid 14 century DR, Sir Ian Glae James Graham wed Lady Holly. To celebrate the event, the happy couple exchanged wedding gifts. The bride used stone shape to shape red marble into a sword and had it decorated with silver and dragon leather, as well as imbued with powerful enchantments. Eventually, Sir Graham met his doom fighting a group of clerics of Talos. As he lay dying in a ravine, Tyr decided to reward the paladin by merging his soul with Red Death. However, Sir Graham saw Tyr's reward as a dire punishment.

Ten years later, a drunken Talassan priest was heard bragging of a paladin's death, and the rumor reached Holly Graham, who ran a cidery outside of Ravens Bluff's walls. She located a band of trustworthy adventurers and asked them to investigate the rumors and find out her long-lost husband's fate.

The adventurers followed the rumors of Talassan activity as it led them to the same ravine where Sir Graham died. Subsequently, he heroes defeated the evil priests with Red Death's help and returned the sentient weapon to Holly. Once the bastard sword met Holly's hand, her lost husband appeared in flesh and blood, looking like he had not aged in a decade.

## Red Sword (mercenary company)

The Red Sword was a mercenary company of 2,000 members that was active in northeast Faerûn sometime before the 14 century DR.

Relationships
The company was known to work for the Red Wizards of Thay, although it was speculated that the Red Wizards hired them solely because "Red" was also in the company's name.

History
When the Simbul learned of the Red Sword's connection to her enemies, the Red Wizards, she and her entourage intercepted them at Mistbridge under a flag of truce in hopes of convincing them not to attack her land of Aglarond. The mercenaries instead insulted the Simbul repeatedly, and ultimately fired a volley of arrows at her and her allies. Losing her temper, the Simbul flew into the air wreathed in flames and—without any assistance—annihilated all 2,000 mercenaries with her spells. The story of what happened to the Red Sword was the single best-known tale about the Simbul as of the mid–14 century DR, and it had made her power (and her temper) famous and feared across Faerûn.

Recken the Bold was a traveling priest from a religious order dedicated to Ilmater, who lived in the 9 century DR.

History
Recken the Bold was the second recipient of the Three White Doves, a powerful enchanted mace created by the priest Alledec in the . The mace was given to Recken the Bold by a certain Ilmatiri fighter. In turn, Recken lost the Three White Doves in a struggle against a powerful vampire near the Trollbark Forest. ## Relve Langral

Relve Langral was a blueflame ghost in the late 15 century DR, once an adventurer among the Nine, in the early 14 century DR.

Personality
He was a little more talkative than Treth Halonter.

Possessions
He carried a sword, even as a ghost.

Description
He was shorter than Treth Halonter; while a blueflame ghost, he smiled constantly. When Marlin couldn't see him, his face showed open contempt.

Relationships
While human, Relve Langral had been one of the Nine, a band of adventurers led by Laeral Silverhand. As a blueflame ghost, he was controlled by Marlin Stormserpent.

Abilities
When a blueflame ghost, Relve Langral could walk through walls.

History
Relve Langral was a thief among the Nine. After the Crown of Horns corrupted them, only one thief in the band survived until : Arnthiir Windriiv, all but stating that Relve Langral had died prior to that moment in time.

While a blueflame ghost, Relve Langral claimed to have been a thief among the Nine, reduced to madness before death by the evil Myrkul and then transformed into a blueflame ghost upon it. He had been called forth at some point prior to 1479 DR. This calling could only have happened prior to 1437 DR as well, as the item containing him, the Flying Blade, had been largely unreachable for anyone but Marlin Stormserpent at least that long.

After testing the command to return to the item in the Dragonskull Chamber at Obarskyr Castle, Marlin commanded him and fellow blueflame ghost Treth Halonter go find and kill Seszgar Huntcrown in Suzail. In executing the command, the two went on a rampage. Every time they met someone on the streets, Relve asked if they'd seen Seszgar Huntcrown, with Treth adding immediately if they'd seen him recently. The duo killed anyone who didn't know where he was. Their rampage ended when they came across Belgryn Murenstur; they intimidated the man into leading them to the Bold Archer. Once there, Relve had Belgryn scout as to the presence of Seszgar; the man confirmed that he remained inside, as well as volunteering the number of bodyguards, and the many other nobles and retinues present inside. The blueflame ghosts walked inside, and killed many people inside, including Seszgar.

After slaying the Huntcrown heir, the two took his corpse to Marlin Stormserpent at Stormserpent Towers; he called on them to throw it in the furnace. They were called on afterwards to kill Jharakphred, an artist for hire, after the later painted for Marlin the likenesses of Draskos Crownsilver and Gariskar Dauntinghorn, who were to be the ghosts' next target. Later, a frightened Marlin used the ghosts to watch over him in his sleep.

On the evening after the disastrous Council of the Dragon, Treth Halonter and Relve Langral were sent into the palace as part of another plan, this time hatched by Marlin Stormserpent and Traevyn Illance both, in which Marlin would make himself out to be the one who stopped those ghosts. Along the way, however, they were spotted by one of the Lords of Waterdeep, Mirt, by the understeward Corleth Fentable, by the evil archmage vampire Manshoon, who was riding on Corleth Fentable's mind at the moment, and finally by the renegade wizard Elminster Aumar. The ghosts were attacked before reaching the palace.

Relve Langral was almost destroyed by one of Elminster's spells, and fled, returning to Marlin Stormserpent, who allowed the two ghosts to rest, on Halonter's advice; the ghost was quite distressed about the injuries they'd received. His next use of the ghosts was to try and capture him some breakfast in the wilderness after he fled Suzail; unfortunately for him, the only creature they were able to kill was a bear, as everything else fled the unnatural entities.

The Flying Blade was seized by Cymmarra after she slew Marlin Stormserpent; she was building her own collection of those ghosts, which she mobilized towards the Bold Blazon at the Festival of Handras. She later pursued Elminster into a secret cave after he retrieved the belt buckle of Lord Calantar (Lord), secretly a blueflame item, wielding her retinue of ghosts against him there. She stopped using the blueflame items after The Simbul, restored by the belt buckle, slew her.

## Red Fang Marauders

The Red Fang Marauders, or simply the Red Fang, was a goblin and goblinoid tribe of marauding bandits, secretly allied with the Zhentarim, that menaced the Western Heartlands in the late 14 century DR.

History

In the late 14 century DR, the Red Fang Marauders targeted the environs of Baldur's Gate, striking at traveling caravans from a base that was well hidden from the Gate's guards. One of the marauders' lairs was hidden in a cave within the Trollbark Forest from where they raided the Trade Way. One of the caravans that fell under the goblinoid attack was Randalla Brasshorn's. She had led the caravan from Waterdeep to Baldur's Gate but it was attacked on the portion of the road south of Dragonspear Castle. That hideout was led by a goblin chieftain Bile-Tooth and housed kidnapped caravaners, including Randalla Brasshorn. Another goblin force set out for the Wayfork Village to pillage and ravage under the leadership of hobgoblin Harnak the Butcher.

Serendipitously, a group of adventurers came across Keaira, Randalla's caravan guard, and set out to find the Trollbark caves. Subsequently, they slew Bile-Tooth and freed the captured caravaners. Soon after, they defended Wayfork Village from the Red Fang Marauders and slew Harnak at the Wayfork Inn.

Subsequently, the adventurers tracked the Red Fang Marauders' to a cave lair in Cloak Wood and fought their way through the goblinoids, discovering the identity of the power behind the bandits - the mysterious woman called the Red Queen, clad in crimson heavy armor. When she was defeated, the Red Queen fled though a secret passage. The armored woman fled to her mistress Arogazia, who resided in the Firewind Manor. Arogazia revealed that the Red Queen was an abomination created by Luvia Bloodmire of Baldur's Gate. But eventually, with the Red Queen's demise, and Firewind Manor being consumed by a blaze, the Red Fang Marauders were no more.

## Resurrectionist

The Resurrectionist was an elderly human man who dwelt in the Shadowfell city of Evernight as of the late 15 century DR. He was one of the city's few living inhabitants.

Description
He was old and frail, and he dressed in a tattered cloak with a hood.

Abilities
The Resurrectionist claimed to be able to bring anyone back from the dead regardless of how damaged or ancient their remains (conditions which constrained normal rites of resurrection). He was generally willing to offer his services to revive someone who had died, but rather than money, he only did so in exchange for some unspecified future favor or quest. Failure to fulfill whatever service he requested would cause the beneficiary of his resurrection magic to instantly drop dead, a fact that he did not keep secret.

Activities
Everyday he would peruse Evernight's Corpse Market for hours, inspecting (and occasionally sniffing) various body parts and always being audibly disappointed in whatever he found. If asked, he said he was looking for any trace of a friend whom he had lost long ago, and who he intended to resurrect despite them having been dead for far longer than a normal resurrection could accommodate. He refused to elaborate about this deceased friend, and seemed to perhaps not even know or remember any details about them.

History
As of, his activities had made him a minor celebrity at the Corpse Market.

## Renault Abrecht

Renault Abrecht was a priest of Bane and the commander of the Thayan garrison at Veil in the late 15 century DR.

Possessions
He wore chainmail and carried a mace.

Abilities
He stayed close to his subordinates in combat, fighting with mace and spell while waiting for the perfect moment to call down dark blessings to heal his allies and harm his enemies.

Activities
Renault operated out of the temple in Veil. As the Thayan commander, he held court there, and as a Banite priest, he held services there and endeavored to convert the village's inhabitants (both living and undead). His true objective was to convert these people to be loyal to him personally.

Relationships
He conspired to stage a coup against the local Thayan leaders of Veil and Surcross, and he was not opposed to forming alliances with other subversive elements within these places, such as those aligned with the self-proclaimed "zulkir in exile" Dempharis Sibront.

History
Renault saw an opportunity to gain more converts when Veil came under attack by the Netherese enclave Kolthunral in the, which had led to dire circumstances in the village. He dreamt of amassing an army of converts whom he would lead down the Shadowfell Road to find some unsuspecting place in Faerûn to invade. On his way out, he further intended to collapse the Shadow Veil, the massive planar rift used by his Thayan overlords to access the Shadowfell Road.

## Relsforin

Relsforin was a hamlet in Thay. As of the late 15 century DR, it was the only community to have survived within the gruesome landscape of the Charnel Fields west of Surcross.

Geography
The community lay west of Surcross within the Bonedark Hills, a contested region on the border of Thay and Thesk. This area was darkened by the looming cliffs of the Thayan Plateau and eruptions from the Thaymount. After the formation of the Charnel Fields, the area around the village was inundated with blood and corpses, reeked of death, and was a hotbed for filth fever.

History
When the Netherese enclave of Kolthunral arrived in the area in the, to attack Surcross, the initial battles between the Netherese and Thayans created the charnel fields and obliterated all other villages in the area. Relsforin endured thanks to Thayan protection, which was offered in exchange for the manual labor of collecting corpses from the Charnel Fields to be used by Thayan necromancers. The villagers were responsible for finding bodies in good physical condition and for gathering and loading them onto military vehicles for transport.

Inhabitants
The people of Relsforin were traumatized by their experiences and terrified of both the Netherese and the Thayans. They feared what would happen should they ever lose the protection of Thay, and so they strove to make themselves useful and were reflexively hostile to any outsiders. They were known to scavenge the valuables from the corpses that they collected as the Thayans were not interested in anything but the bodies.

## Reldegar's Roost

Reldegar's Roost was a rough tavern and inn located in the Sembian city of Yhaunn, owned by the retired sailor Barnacle Bill.

Location
The establishment was found on the city's docks, where desperate sailors might easily find it.

Atmosphere

Reldegar's was a filthy and poorly maintained place that permanently smelled of rum, with almost everything inside it stained or scarred. The furniture was universally made of thick, durable wood. Bill didn't tolerate any horseplay and kept the peace with a hefty club with a bronze head, which was kept mounted with hooks above the bar below a sign stating "No Fighting! Take it Outside!"

Services
The Roost was considered perhaps the worst establishment in Yhaunn, with its only saving grace being its low prices. Bill stayed out of his customer's business whenever possible, focusing on food, drink, and lodgings over any rumor-mongering or gossip-brokering.

History
In the 1360s DR, adventurers on a treasure hunt encountered the pirates Rufus the Cruel and Malificat at the Roost, as well as the Cormyrean privateers Seldar and Throxis.

The Region of Dreams was a plane coterminous with the Prime Material plane, to which mortal beings traveled out-of-body whenever they dreamed. The Region of Dreams was said to be coterminous with the Demiplane of Nightmares.

## Realms of Shadow/Assassin's Shadow

"Assassin's Shadow" is a short story by Jess LeBow that appeared in the Realms of Shadow anthology of stories from the Forgotten Realms, published in April 2002.

Summary

In a musty cellar Olostin met with a veiled figure, they reminisced about Olostin's successes over the years thanks to the knowledge and power the figure gave him. Olostin vowed to end the archwizard's rule, and the figure asked him to eliminate the archwizard Shadow before he could learn more about the Shadow Weave and become a significant threat. The action shifted to Cy, one of Olostin's raiders. During an attack on the Low Netherese village of Kath, Cy killed a powerful, chain-wielding man. Having seen his valor, Cy's commander Lume recruited him to assassinate Shadow. Cy took a two day long griffon ride to the floating city Karsus. He failed to assassinate Shadow but learned Shadow had a powerful wand, before he escaped while another group attempted to kill Shadow. When he returned to Lume, the commander revealed he had sent countless others to accomplish the same task, and Cy was the only one ever to return. They went together to try again, against Cy's better judgment. Lume stole and used the wand, but it backfired and killed him instead of Shadow.

Note on the Date

Olostin was said to have perished from incineration by a fireball cast at higher than 34th level in -477 DR, 10 years prior to the date in the story. No one in the story acknowledged his death, but rather talked as if he was alive. There is not a clear, single, correct way to rectify this discrepancy.

## Relchoz Hriiat

Relchoz Hriiat was a baker in the city of Waterdeep in the 14 century DR, owner and operator of Hriiat Fine Pastries, as well as the public contact for the city's Baker's Guild.The book Adventurer's Guide to the City in the City of Splendors boxed-set states that the Hriiat Fine Pastries is run by "the master of the Bakers' Guild". ## Realms of the Elves/The Staff of Valmaxian

"The Staff of Valmaxian" is a short story by Philip Athans first published in the 2006 anthology Realms of the Elves.

Summary
In  in the gold elven realm of Siluvanede, Valmaxian is a young gold elf mage who has trouble mastering the creation of magic items. His frustration and thirst for magic leads him to steal one of his master's scrolls to summon the demon En'Sel'Diren. Valmaxian asks for magic, and En'Sel'Diren agrees to grant it, for a price none of them yet knows.

Six hundred and sixty four years later, Valmaxian is the most sought-after and pompous magic item crafter, when he hears that his old master, Kelaerede, is on his deathbed and has sent for him. Master and student had not spoken to each other since Valmaxian's bargain with the devil, but the student still visited his master. Kelaerede wanted Valmaxian to admit that his ways were flawed and repent in order to rest in peace, something that Valmaxian himself did not agree upon, but he finally gave in after the prompting of Chasianna, Kelaerede's granddaughter.

Two years later, Valmaxian and Chasianna are now lovers and Valmaxian is working on his masterpiece staff. His demon magic is not enough to complete it, though, while Chasianna's magic of self-sacrifice fares better on it. For that, Valmaxian summons En'Sel'Diren once more, but before striking a new bargain, En'Sel'Diren asks for Chasianna as payment for their previous bargain. Valmaxian refuses, but his magic is not enough to stop the demon from taking her anyway. Valmaxian follows them into the Abyss, and after fighting off En'Sel'Diren's minions he faces the demon himself. In the end, he has to sacrifice his masterpiece staff and his demon magic in order to save him and his lover from the demon's clutches.

## Realms of the Elves/Necessary Sacrifices

His induction at the Oak of Shevarash in Cormanthyr includes membership to the Dark Avengers. The Dark Avengers were made up of elves of all kinds who sought vengeance and retribution on the drow for personal losses.

Shortly after his induction on Midwinter night of the Year of Moonfall, 1344 DR, there is a drow assault close by. The council of Cormanthyr calls for retaliation via the Dark Avengers.

## Realms of the Elves/The Greater Treasure

"The Greater Treasure" is a short story by Erik Scott de Bie first published in the 2006 anthology Realms of the Elves.

Summary
“The Greater Treasure” takes place in the month of Eleasias in 1362 DR, the Year of the Helm, in the city of Elversult on the Dragon Coast. The sun elf Yldar Nathalan and his wizardess sister, Cythara, both exiled from House Nathalan, or the House of the Crescent Bow, in Evermeet, arrive in Elversult just as the Scarred Eagle adventuring band defeats the Cowled Skull dynasty of Elversult, and the lady mage Yanseldara is crowned in the Skulls' place; all the while the citizens of Elversult go about their daily business. Yldar is searching for an ancient magical elven relic known as Ynloeth's Bracer, which was once worn by Coronal Ynloeth of Shantel Othreier, a hero of the Crown Wars.

While in the Splitskull inn in Elversult, Yldar and Cythara meet a beautiful and humorous moon elf thief who goes by the name Fox-at-Twilight. She is later revealed to be a shadowdancer (she can walk through shadows) and a former lover of Erevan Ilesere, the Fey Jester, the elf god of mischief and rogues. Twilight agrees to help them obtain the bracer, and she takes them to the House of Coins, a temple of Waukeen, Lady of Merchants and goddess of commerce. The basement of the temple is used by the Deep Coven, a cabal that worships the demon prince Graz'zt, who has been holding Waukeen captive since the Godswar. While in the temple of Graz'zt, Cythara, seeking more power and tired of following in her younger brother's footsteps, betrays Yldar and Twilight. Twilight takes the bracer, and she and Yldar flee, leaving Cythara behind with the demon worshipers. Cythara defeats the lamia Leis'anna, leader of the Deep Coven and Chosen of Graz'zt, and becomes the new leader and Chosen.

Yldar and Twilight fall in love. Yldar asks Twilight to help him save his sister, but Twilight says he cannot ask that of her. When Yldar awakens the next day, after the last night he and Twilight lay together, Twilight is gone. She leaves with him the Bracer of Ynloeth and a note of parchment reading, “Farewell, and remember.”

## Realms of the Elves/Comrades at Odds

"Comrades at Odds" is a short story by R.A. Salvatore first published in the 2006 anthology Realms of the Elves.

Summary
Tos'un Armgo finds himself without any allies since his three drow companions are all dead and he has become an enemy of King Obould Many-Arrows because of Kaer'lic Suun Wett's attempt to kill the orc while Obould and Drizzt Do'Urden were fighting. Tos'un knows that he cannot survive the journey back through the Underdark alone. Feeling helpless on the surface world, he lets out his rage by attacking orcs with the powerful sentient blade Khazid'hea.

On their journey to recover the body of Ellifain, Drizzt Do'Urden and Innovindil find that the orc hordes have not yet disbanded after the (so Drizzt and Innovindil believe) death of King Obould. Upon inspection, the two elves find that the orcs are building a gate upon a trail further fortifying the orc kingdom. Realizing there is nothing they can do, they continue their journey.

Tos'un Armgo becomes more powerful and dangerous under the subtle tutelage of Khazid'hea. In the battles the drow wages on unsuspecting orcs, the blade imparts the thoughts of "do not think", instilling to Tos'un to rely on his instincts and training without allowing his conscious thoughts to impede him. This was what Khazid'hea learned from Drizzt in the short time he wielded the blade.

The elves of the Moonwood find another scene where orcs were slain by Tos'un. Sinnafain and Albondiel discuss whether Tos'un is good or evil and that they will treat him accordingly.

Drizzt and Innovindil find and recover the body of Ellifain whereupon Innovindil performs a ritual asking the elven gods to rescue Ellifain from her anger. Innovindil comforts Drizzt and assures him that they will convene with the spirit of Ellifain upon their return to the Moonwood to ease Drizzt's own guilt.

As Tos'un is about to finish slaying an orc encampment, one orc falls to elven arrows. He meets with Sinnafain but she shows no signs of aggression towards him. Khazid'hea implores him to kill her but he resists knowing the he would have a dozen arrows in him before his first attack. An elven priest casts a spell to converse with the dark elf as well as a spell to detect lies. Khazid'hea assists Tos'un to deceive the latter spell. After the encounter, the blade tries to convince Tos'un to leave the elves but the drow knows he has no where else to go.

Drizzt and Innovindil return to the Moonwood where they encounter a patrol of elves. They recount their story to the patrol and also learn that King Obould is still alive and also find out that Tos'un Armgo is now residing in the Moonwood with the elves.

Upon finding out that Drizzt is in the Moonwood, Khazid'hea once again tries to convince Tos'un to leave. The blade tells him that Drizzt will recognize from the battle with Obould and that at the very least he would take Khazid'hea back since it previously belonged to Catti-brie. Tos'un agrees to leave and the blade tells him to kill Sinnafain who is the elf near to his position. Tos'un strikes her to the ground and puts the blade to her chin but does not kill her. He tells her that he is not her enemy but Drizzt would not understand. After disarming her and moving farther from the camp of elves, he forces Sinnafain to the ground once again and runs away.

When Drizzt and Innovindil meet up with Sinnafain and she tells him of Tos'un's escape, he vows to find him. Later, a priest performs a ritual which allows Ellifain's spirit to possess Innovindil's body. Innovindil's body walks to the hut in which Drizzt is residing in, kneels next to him and confirms that she is the spirit of Ellifain. She embraces him and tells that now she knows the truth. After the spirit of Ellifain leaves, Innovindil returns to a Drizzt free of guilt and ready to face the obstacles before him.

## Realms of the Elves/The Bladesinger's Lesson

"The Bladesinger's Lesson" is a short story by Richard Baker first published in the 2006 anthology Realms of the Elves.

Summary
Daried Selsherryn an Elven Bladesinger of some nobility is commanding a defensive scouting party in his forsaken homeland of Cormanthyr against a Sembian invading force. The elven troop is small and posted there by higher command for fear of a stealthy flanking of the larger elven force that they are apart of. There is some question as to the value of their post as the River Ashaba was thought to be extremely difficult to ford.

While in the area Daried can not resist visiting the abandoned estate of his family's previous home. When entering the gardens he finds the protective wards countered, and evidence of tomb robbing perhaps decades prior. Of particular frustration to him is the missing Selsherryn Moonblade -  long magically defunct.

He excuses himself from the command of his small troop to enquire in the nearby small human settlement in the vicinity of Mistledale.  Daried considers the community  'nameless' and of no account. There he makes accusations and insinuations on many of the towns folk. When inquiring about the taken  moonblade he finds out that he is just days late - as the house that held the "acquired sword" has been ransacked  just nights before his arrival by a band of mercenaries.

He engages on the hunt for the mercenary band and the moonblade - tracking them to a critical river fording - one of which he was unaware. The hunt for the moonblade has brought him to the realization that there is a weak point - and further more the Sembian's Mercenaries are aware of the river crossing.

Daried stops an archer named Nilsa who has also followed the marauders - she's bent on killing the raiders one arrow at a time. Nilsa is the local descendant of the man who had taken the moon blade and is clearly of questionable heritage.

Together they perform some valiant heroism. Lessons about valor and worth are learned.

## Red musk zombie

Red musk zombies were the remains of humanoids that succumbed to the effects of red musk creepers, separate but related to yellow musk creepers and their zombies.

Description
Red musk zombies resembled lumpen green humanoids with irregular growths and distorted proportions. They were unable to speak and simply clubbed their victims with their misshapen, thorny paws.

Ecology

On exposure to red musk creeper pollen, victims became feverish, confused, erratic, and violent. As it progressed they became deathly ill before succumbing entirely and being overtaken by the plant's form. While patients could be healed and cleansed of the disease in the earlier stage, there was no return from becoming a zombie as the victim was functionally replaced by the plant.

The humanoids turned to zombies were fully consumed by plant-matter and no longer functioned as animals, lacking most of the biological functions, thought processes, and organs inherent to most creatures. They absorbed electrical shocks to an extent, taking less damage and gaining a temporary boost of energy.

The most powerful zombies grew red musk creepers upon their bodies, allowing them to infect others.

History

Red musk creepers and their zombies were first seen after the maddened druid Halsaime deployed them as weapons against the inhabitants of Eveningstar during the drow invasion of the mid-1480s DR. The innocent ranger Peron Uthe carried the infection from Thorngap and quickly succumbed at the Eveningstar War Hospital, transforming into a zombie and infecting other patients. Only the timely intervention of adventurers was able to save the day, along with the head priest Hurim Jaskyr developing an inoculation against the disease that allowed Peron Uthe to be safely destroyed. More zombies were found in his home of Uthe Lodge accompanying the dryad Sylvie, and deep in the Thorngap at Thorn Hollow and Druid's Deep protecting Halsaime.

## Red musk creeper

The red musk creeper was a peculiar and dangerous plant found in Faerûn, related to the yellow musk creeper.

Ecology
The creepers produced a pollen that caused a sickness in humanoids, leading to a period of feverish incapacitation, confusion, and the irreversible transformation into a red musk zombie. The zombie could eventually bloom more flowers and further spread the disease.

History
Red musk creepers and their zombies were first seen during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, when the druid Halsaime infected the ranger Peron Uthe and allowed him to return to Eveningstar. Feeling sick, Peron went to the Eveningstar War Hospital for treatment, only to transform into a red musk zombie and infect many other patients. The head priest Hurim Jaskyr got adventurers to collect samples from the transformed patients Mikhael Jarvin and Aeron Leifbrand to create an inoculation against infection, allowing Peron to be destroyed. A garden's worth of red musk creepers were later found in the basement of Uthe Lodge, growing around the tree of the corrupted dryad Sylvie, who sought to destroy the entire town.Though not explicitly stated, the circumstances around the red musk outbreak imply that Halsaime created or altered the plant in some way to make it more dangerous.

While the mercenaries never returned to Impiltur, their captain, Delros Parander, was subsequently seen wielding the famous royal Impilturan sword known as Malagar, which he presumably discovered while on that expedition.

## Receptacle

Receptacle was a psionic devotion used by psionicists to store power in objects.

Effect
The power could be used on gemstones, with an ability to store energy that was directly proportional to their value, or on vessels that had been prepared through the empower psionic science.

Through the use of this devotion, these could be gradually filled with power over time. Though there was no limit to the receptacles a given psionicist could create, they couldn't more than double their available potential energy by means of that psionic devotion: the total power stored in all of their receptacles could not exceed their personal full power. Investing that amount of power took roughly a couple hours for many psionicists, while particularly powerful ones required twice or more that time.

A psionicist who held a receptacle could call on its stored power for as long as the vessel held potential and was in contact with their skin, allowing them to manifest powers for longer, or more often than they would on their own potential alone. However, damage to the vessel would drain an amount of the psionicist's potential equivalent to the vessel's power currently available for manifesting. It also halved the recovery of their energies until they recovered completely.

Known Users
 Crysmals

## Remichispace

Remichispace was a large star system in wildspace named after the planet Remichi. The system played a major role in the war against the Xaryxian Empire.

Description
The system consisted of one or two stars at its center and at least four planets: Beloria, Byzunoz, Remichi, and a fourth unnamed planet. The system also supported an asteroid belt known as Basalt's Belt, which contained the asteroid Omomia. Also situated within the system was the asteroid Zar'bos. The system possessed few if any large settlements and was largely populated with temporary inhabitants and non-sentient creatures.

The Solar System
Baloria
Baloria was a lush and rocky Toril-sized earth body located at a comfortable distance for sustaining life and, as a result, exhibited a temperate climate. The world was known for a massive spelljamming ship that crashed onto its surface ages before the late 1400s DR. It was occasionally bombarded with cosmic magic due to a starburst from another nearby system. These cosmic flares could cause passing ships to malfunction and crash onto the surface.

The planet at one point become populated by astral elves loyal to Emperor Xavan, due to a group of Xaryxian defectors, known as the Mutineer Underground, becoming based on the planet after being forced to crash their ships. The loyal astral elves at one point planted an astral seed on the world for destruction, though it was quickly destroyed. Eventually the astral elves left the planet.

Byzunov
Byzunov was a massive ringed spherical earth body covered with sulfur pools that emitted noxious fumes into its atmosphere. The star of the system cast a faint ominous glow on the surface of the world. The carbon planet temporarily became the site of a conflict between a group of space orcs and space elves at war. The orcs and elves reached a state of peace. The astral elves of Xaryxis briefly targeted the world for destruction through the planting of an astral seed on its rocky landscape, before ultimately being stopped.

Basalt's Belt
A string of rocky asteroids, consisting of large basalt deposits. This asteroid belt was formed ages ago from a great collision between two young planets, which caused pieces of cooling magma to fly into wildspace. One of these asteroids was Omomia.

Omomia
Omomia was an irregular earth body mostly consisting of dark colored basalt rock and water pools. it also lacked an atmosphere. A group of astral seafaring priests, worshipers of the Plane of Water, took residence there many years before 1479 DR. They considered the asteroid sacred and was originally named by the goddess Persana. The native inhabitants of the rock were kuo-toa, who were regularly targeted by the priests who lived there. The priests previously worshiped an aboleth who'd called the place home, before murdering it as a result of its aggressive behavior and as a test of its divinity.

Remichi
Remichi was a dry desert earth body located far away from the star of the system. Deep in its two-hundred-year orbit, the planet was regularly covered in darkness, and all moisture had evaporated into space long before, leaving the planet dry. The planet and its native beings were very old. Remains of the original inhabitants' civilization and their structures were still found on the surface. The beings who'd built these were once of flesh before changing over time and evolving to lose their physical form and becoming imperceptible beings. The beings were sometimes extracted of aberration essence, which was sold for exuberant prices and used for energy. The beings on the planet had the magical ability to prevent spelljamming ships from traveling off the planet.

In the late 1400s DR, a group of mercane landed on the world looking for riches to plunder and resources to exploit. Astral elves of the Xaryxian Empire were also seen on the planet and attempted to demolish its ancient ruins for unknown purposes.

Other Astronomical Bodies
Zar'bos
A tidally locked and dimly lit irregular earth body, mostly devoid of life, Zar'bos was located one day's travel to a nearby planet. The asteroid was inhabited by a large colony of mind flayers located on the surface and deep within the body, as well as an unusually large elder brain. A band of githzerai traveled from their home in Limbo to the place in order to eliminate the illithid threat.

Unnamed Planet
A planet that was one day's travel to Zar'bos and hosted a population of many duergar. The population on the planet provided the illithids on Zar'bos with a steady supply of beings to subjugate as thralls.

Inhabitants
A number of notable inhabitants resided or are associated with Remichispace, including:
 General Caila Eanor, commander of the Mutineer Underground.
 Grand Master Nasraka, leader of a group of githzerai who traveled to Zar'bos to eliminate a mind flayer colony.
 Vicar Aenon, notable member of the astral priests of Omomia.
 Qar'zah, a mercane who became stranded on Remichi.

## Repose mantle

The Repose mantle was a psionic mantle that granted ardents and divine minds an array of psionics powers and abilities derived from the universal concepts of rest and tranquility.

Deities

Abilities
Those who followed the repose mantle embodied the concept, finding it easier than others to relax their mind and concentrate. By expending a psionic focus, they could greatly improve their ability to concentrate.

The psychic aura exuded by divine minds of the repose mantle suppressed the negative effects of fatigue in both the manifester and all allies within the radius of the aura. If an ally of the manifester were to fall victim to exhaustion while within the radius of their aura, the manifester would take the burden upon themselves.

Powers
Novice manifesters could calm themselves and others through empty mind and serenity. Manifesters of some experience learned solicit psicrystal and could take on the wounds of others with empathic transfer. As a manifester progressed further they could make themselves immune to illusions with steadfast perception and suspend their bodies with suspend life. Masters of this mantle obtained personal mind blank, allowing them to become impervious to scrying and mental effects.

## Red Shroud

Red Shroud was the succubus sorceress ruler of the Broken Reach, a cast fortress of intrigue and betrayal in Pazunia in the Abyss.

Description
Red Shroud was a beautiful succubus with flame-red hair and pale skin. She had large bat-like wings and her eyes glowed with sinister desire. As a businesswoman, she was capricious and untrustworthy.

Relationships
Red Shroud was Malcanthet's daughter and worked with Shami-Amourae to betray and oust her mother. Over the centuries, she birthed thousands of children. Notably, Red Shroud sold her own daughter, the cleric later known as Fall-from-Grace, into baatezu slavery.

Some rumormongers suggested that Red Shroud's consorts had some sway over her, however, that was unconfirmed and likely false. By the late 14 century DR, she had four consorts since founding Broken Reach. The nalfeshnee named Ygrax the Skullbiter was the last known lover of the succubus's. He was held in line and was fully subservient to Red Shroud, however, it was unknown of it was just an act. At all times, Red Shroud was surrounded by a quartet of hezrou bodyguards. The wispling named Shadowfire was her favorite agent and a secret servant of Demogorgon.

Posesions
Red Shroud armed herself with a powerful dagger of venom. She also used iron bands of Bilarro and a shroud of venom in battle.

Activities
She had a reputation of a deadly poisoner and a source of useful information. She enjoyed dealing in poisons and reveled in toying with the prey using slow-acting venoms.

History
Red Shroud founded Broken Reach more than two hundred years before the late 14 century DR atop the Plague-Mort portal in the Outlands. She ruled the fortress with a harsh harsh and absolute power. She allowed trade and visitors in her domain as long as they were not foolish enough to cross the succubus ruler. She was powerful and dependable enough to protect Broken Reach from mobs, githzerai, and minor Abyssal lords. Her governing with an iron fist ensure that no violence was allowed within the fortress's walls and the absolute rule applied to the inhabitants and guests alike.

## Red Griffon Room

The Red Griffon Room was a chamber in the High Palace of Silverymoon during the 14 century DR that contained a dead magic zone.

Interior
The room was stately, its walls lined with duskwood panels. There was a polished table for meetings, and many chairs; the chairposts were decorated with scarlet-colored carved griffons.

The room was very stuffy, with no way in or out beyond the door. A copper heat-turned fan hung from a curved rod, over a candle-lamp, acting as the only ventilation available.

Defenses
The room was built around a dead magic zone, and thus magic did not really work in there, with very few exceptions known to include some abilities of the Chosen of Mystra. To verify the zone wasn't tampered with, there was a coin hidden in one hollow carving that held a burning spell. If the spell was ever able to resume, the coin would burn away.

History
The room was created after a wizardly duel following a MageFair led to the inception of a dead-magic zone inside one of the palace's rooms. After praying to Mystra, Alustriel decided to preserve the zone rather than remedy it. The room was rebuilt and divided in two, with the dead-magic zone becoming the Red Griffon Room.

The trader Garthin Muirtree requested the Red Griffon Room in the  for a meeting with Auvrarn Labraster, whom he heard employed a "sneaking magic". But he was mysteriously murdered in that same room; he was recorded to have also met on the day before his death with Dauphran Alskyte, Draevin Flarwood, Janthasarde Ilbright, and Oscalar Maerbree.

## Reaver (creature)

Reavers were a race of aquatic creatures from the Domains of Dread who on rare occasions intruded into Toril's waters to plague fishermen and others living near the shore.

Description
Reavers looked like tall humanoid creatures covered with small razor-sharp scales. They had large fish-like eyes and webbed hands and feet, with the fingers and toes ending in short but deadly sharp claws that could rip through flesh and bone with ease. Their mouths were wide and fish-like and filled with rows of needle-like teeth. They spoke with a lisping, hissing tongue in a language that was very difficult for other races to match. Some of the sounds they used to communicate were ultrasonic, so most could not even hear them.

Personality
As a race, the reavers were an evil and dark people, who lurked near the shore hoping to get close to swimmers, fishermen, or small boats they would then ambush, dragging their victims down into the deeps. They were carnivorous to a fault, eating only the raw flesh of land-dwelling creatures or sea mammals. Likewise intelligent prey were seen as far better meat than that of simple animal life. Thus a reaver would often pass up other targets to strike at a wandering band of humans or demihumans. After feasting on the bodies of their victims, they would leave behind a grisly trophy of blood and death, marking their successful hunt and warning off those who might think of hunting them down for vengeance.

Combat
Not noted for their clever tactics and intricate strategies, the reaver would be a brutal and savage opponent that tore its victim into pieces.
When in mêlée, the reaver would strike thrice, with both its claw-like hands and with its deadly bite. It would attempt to grapple the victim and drag them along its scales, using their sharpness to cut them up even more. Needless to say, such close encounters usually ended badly for those held by a reaver. Soft weapons like whips were also in danger of being destroyed if used on a reaver as those sharp scales would cut the weapons even as they were struck by them.

Society
Reavers tended to gather in schools like fish, of a dozen or so individuals. Territorial in the extreme, they often regarded any settlements near their lairs as intrusions upon their territory, regardless of whether or not those settlements predated the lair's establishment. Any such 'intrusions' were rewarded with night-raids on the settlement, with each of these raids marked by violent acts of terror against a specific household. They did this hoping to drive off the inhabitants from the lands that bordered on the reavers' ocean realm.

Reaver lairs were often hidden beneath a coral reef or at the heart of a thick seaweed forest, with reavers stealthily hunting and slaying those who stumbled upon the location of the lair, or even just intruded near it.

From time to time, a reaver would be exiled from its group, usually due to failing some important task. These outcasts would leave the ocean behind and find a freshwater river or lake to take up a solitary existence preying on settlements otherwise far removed from the usual reaver habitats.

History
In the  in the Weathercote Woods, the adventurers Florozt'a, Galimer, Tiep, and Druhallen were ambushed by a reaver in the middle of the night.

## Read thoughts (psionic)

Read thoughts, also known as detect thoughts, extrasensory perception, or ESP for short, was a psionic devotion of the telepathy discipline that allowed a manifester to perceive a target's surface thoughts.

Effects
A manifester was able to perceive the surface or "active" thoughts of beings within range. Older versions of the power had an unlimited range, while in the late 14 century DR its range was limited to, emanating outward in a quarter circle or cone-shape from the manifester. Read thoughts could work through barriers, though couldn't penetrate through certain depths, including  of stone,  of common metal, a thin sheet of lead, and  feet of dirt or wood. It had a duration of about 1 or more minutes and required concentration from the manifester.

How long the manifester examined a particular area or subject determined what manner of information they could gleam from their surface thoughts. The instinctual and picturesque thoughts of simple minds, such as those of animals, were easy to pick up. An affected mind's location could also be pinpointed by the manifester, but only if they were within line of sight. Attempting to read the surface thoughts of a creature with god-like intellect would instantaneously disrupt the manifestation and temporarily stun the manifester.

Older versions of ESP were limited by language, perceiving the thoughts of intelligent creatures as words. The magical thought processes used by wizards and other spellcasters as part of spellcasting were also unintelligible, though they would be easily recognizable for what they were. And if a manifestation backfired, the manifester would be struck with a splitting headache and difficulty using any telepathic powers for the next hour.

Prerequisite Powers
This power once required contact as a prerequisite.

Displays
When used this power typically manifested a mental display, but in some cases manifested a visual display as well.

Significance
ESP was among the many telepathic powers that could be guarded against with conceal thoughts and mind blank. It was also a prerequisite for probe, a power that allowed a manifester to dig deeper into a target's thoughts.

Users
This power was largely exclusive to psions specialized as telepaths, but through personal meditation cognition thieves gained access to it and on occasion it manifested among wild talents.

Read thoughts was exhibited as an innate psionic ability by amethyst dragons, brain coral, brainstealer dragons of old or greater age, crysmals, dhours, elder brains, emerald dragons, many illithids and half-illithids, adult intellect devourers, kigrids, neothelids, psurlons, some sulls, ulitharids, urophions, and yochlol.

In psionics terms, it could be known by couatl, hollyphants, and ki-rin.

Known/Notable Users
 Belmar Ornan, a Turmishan wild talent.
 Eldenser, an amethyst great wyrm.
 Kimmuriel Oblodra, a drow psion.
 Lugribossk, a proxy of the illithid god Ilsensine.
 Malaeragoth, a very old sapphire dragon.
 Saryndalaghlothtor, an adult crystal dragon.
 Winestab, a human thief and psionic wild talent.

## Red Queen

The Red Queen was the mysterious armored woman whose gauntleted fist was behind the Red Fang Marauders's raids on merchants and traders around Baldur's Gate in the late 14 century DR.

Description
The Red Queen's appearance was hidden by a full-body set of  plate mail with impressive pauldrons and a closed helmet, all made of crimson metal. She wielded a long sword made out of the same material.

History
The Red Queen was not a natural-born creature. She was a construct created by Luvia Bloodmire of Baldur's Gate, a mad mage who conducted sinister experiments, crafting undead and construct creatures by melding her victims' bodies and various exotic black market parts. Some of Luvia's work was for hire and the Red Queen was one such creation, made in Arogazia Firewind's image. She was to serve Arogazia, who resided in the Firewind Manor within the Cloak Wood.

Arogazia Firewind, secretly a shape-changed red dragon Aizagora, was an ally of the Zhentarim, who tried to obtain the power of the Onyx Tower and bring ruination to Baldur's Gate. She tasked her loyal Red Queen to assemble and lead a force of goblinoids under the banner of the Red Fang Marauders, attacking merchants, traders, travelers, and caravans in the vicinity of Baldur's Gate. Out of her cave lair in the Cloak Wood, the Red Queen led the Marauders until a group of adventurers came across the Wayfork Village being attacked by the Red Fang. The adventurers tracked the raiders to the Queen's lair and defeated her in battle. Still alive, the Red Queen fled through a secret passage to her mistress's forest manse. But the adventurers followed and found out the Red Queen's true master, Arogazia. Subsequently, the Red Queen met her demise, and Firewind Manor was consumed by a blaze, the Red Fang Marauders were no more.

## Red Wizard's sight

The Red Wizard's sight was a set of magical goggles found in Myth Drannor during the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Imbued with a certain vision for the future, these goggles aided a spellcaster with their magic, with particular focus on blasting foes with force or bludgeoning them with blunter methods.

History
The goggles were found after the baelnorn Phathroi had the Elves of Cormanthor raid a once-lost Cult of the Dragon base, where the Red Wizards of Kathul Sep performed necromantic rites upon the dracolich Karliath.

## Relig

Relig was a powerful wizard and necromancer in the service of House Szind during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the 1480s DR.

Description
Relig had messy mid-length white hair, and he wore an elaborate armored robe.

Personality

Relig was rather grandiose and arrogant, having supreme belief in his abilities. As a professional megalomaniacal necromancer, he was completely callous towards the suffering of others, seeing them only as tools to advance himself with.

Abilities

Relig claimed to be the most extraordinary necromancer in history, but his abilities weren't quite a match. He was still a powerful spellcaster and a masterful necromancer, and knew deadly spells like chain lightning, curse, finger of death, horrid wilting, Niac's biting cold, and symbol of weakness.It is unclear whether Relig had a realistic chance of creating perfect undead, or if he was entirely deluded regarding his own abilities

Possessions
Relig possessed a powerful staff of the necromancer (not that one).

History

Relig was the head wizard of House Szind, which largely involved mass amounts of necromancy for the house's business interests. During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, rumors reached the surface of Szind developing new and terrifying undead—a project spearheaded by Relig. He was working to create a new form of undead that was completely physically and mentally intact, making them indistinguishable from the living while still remaining under the full control of a necromancer. While he had grand promises and seemed to believe them, Relig was unable to reach these goals and was dependent on more traditional necromantic rituals and the house's necromantic cauldrons to make undead of any quality. The Amaunatori priestess Oryne Belamadin considered this to be an unacceptable threat, and so sent a team down disguised as drow to sabotage House Szind and end the threat by any means.

Relig conspired with the apprentice Sarnish and the priestess Silan Szind to kill and reanimate her mother, Matron Zhivsha Szind. While Silan thought she was in charge of the plot, Relig secretly considered himself her master and destined to rule not just House Szind but the whole city as well. When heroes from Cormyr broke into House Szind to ruin his plans, he had his personal guards stand down and welcome them into his ritual chamber, letting the surfacers bask in his glory. Unfortunately for him, even with his guards and undead minions the invaders were able to slay him, putting an end to Szind's fell business.

## Reaver (heavy flail)

Reavers were a variety of enchanted heavy flails created on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR, and used as a valuable smuggling good on the Elemental Plane of Fire.

Powers
There existed three variants of the Reavers weapon, each possessed a +1, +2, and +8 enchantments what made the flail's bludgeons deadlier and guided the wielder's hand towards the target. All three versions were ensorcelled to deal additional magical cold damage upon strikes.

An ironwood mace could be created from an enchanted heavy flail and a portion of dragon blood.

History
The oldest and strongest variety of the Reaver flails were created to be smuggled to the Elemental Plane of Fire by planeswalking travelers and merchants tho sought to make extra coin. Theses weapons were deadly to the creatures of fire and were perfect tools for the Plane of Fire's assassins.

More common versions of Reavers with a simpler +1 enchantment were forged by Master Blacksmith Marrok of Shining Knight Arms and Armor shop of Neverwinter to help the heroes who sought the cure from the dreaded Wailing Death of the . Later the same year, more powerful versions were created by Master Blacksmith Barun of Beorunna's Well to help the forces of Neverwinter stand against Lady Aribeth the Betrayer, who led a marching army of Luskan in the deadly but short-lived Neverwinter-Luskan war.

## Revenge among Thieves

"Revenge Among Thieves" is a short story by Ed Greenwood, originally an outtake from a chapter in "Elminster: The Making of a Mage".

Summary
Now the sole survivor of the Velvet Hands, Elminster decides to take revenge on the Moonclaws rival gang. Unknown to him, the Magister of Mystra, Dorgon Heamiiolothtar, has Elminster's friend, the thief Farl with him, alive and unharmed. The Magister kisses the dead body of Tassabra, another thief friend slain by the Moonclaws, and she awakens to life.

Elminster goes to the hiding place of the Velvet Hands, and discovers Orluth Angalaer, one of the Magelords of Athalantar, lying in ambush. He manages to surprise and slay the wizard using Tassabra's ring of the ram while he's busy boasting. Then he attacks Isparla, shooting one of her bodyguards in his head; they pursue and he lures them into a trapped warehouse, where his traps slay many before they decide to set the warehouse on fire. He barely manages to escape through a smuggler's tunnel, and sets Isparla's house on fire.

Isparla and the rest of the Moonclaws head over to their hideout, the Roost of the Moonclaws, in an uptown bath-house. Elminster follows them there, and kills one. Then some spell freezes them all in time, and a voice tells Elminster not to touch any foes, and that Tassabra and Farl are alive.

Index

Appendix
Background
In the 2000 book "Secrets of the Magister", a segment of the short story was reproduced. In the book, it was presented as part of a deleted chapter from Elminster: The Making of a Mage, entitled "Dark Times in Hastarl".

## Ren Haldine

Possessions
He owned the Haldine farm.

Relationships
His grand-daughter was Fatrina Haldine.

History
Ren Haldine was the owner of the Haldine farm during the mid-to-late 14 century DR, at the same time that Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls was turned undead by the vampire Romanisk Vlaensk. Once undead, Vilhelm's reign became cruel, erratic, and terrible. When he sent his guards to bring him Ren Haldine, patriarch of the Haldine, and Ren's grand-daughter Fatrina Haldine, whom he fancied, the entire Haldine farm fled into nearby caves.

## Reptile bane cudgel

The reptile bane cudgel was the traditional weapon of the Lannon family of the Mere of Dead Men on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR.

Description
It was a club made of iron.

Powers
The club possessed a +1 enchantment that made the cudgel deadlier and guided the wielder's hand toward the target. Additionally, it enhanced the user's ability to hit lizardfolk.

History
Some time before the, the Lannon family, swamp dwellers living near the village of West Harbor, came into possession of it. When lizardfolk from the swamp attacked, the Lannons were forced to flee their home. There they met a stranger, whom they asked for help. To support the adventurer (if they agreed to help), Pitney Lannon, the father of the family, gave them the reptile bane cudgel. Afterward, Pitney recalled that, as far as he knew, it had never been used and he wasn't sure if it even was magical, as his father had a reputation as a liar.

The bite marks in Revans's neck suggested a vampire, though one was not responsible.

## Realms of Magic/Gunne Runner

"Gunne Runner" is a short story by Roger E. Moore that appeared in the Realms of Magic anthology of stories from the Forgotten Realms, published in December 1995.

Summary
In Waterdeep, the wizard Formathio and watch captain Ardrun investigate the murder of the Yellow Mage and uncover a smokepowder-weapon smuggling operation. They find it's connected to a small scro spelljamming fleet that arrived in Realmspace and is and trading firearms to humanoid races in Faerûn through contacts in Waterdeep.

## Renton Finne

Renton Finne was a Harper agent who worked in Eveningstar, Cormyr, in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Renton was notable for his very bald head, and large lute.

History

After the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon during the 1480s DR, Renton joined Kara Patwin and Lorant Tass in investigating the Storm Horns and Amskar's army of monsters. Once it was clear that an enormous army was gathered with fell intent, Renton found that a key frost giant leader, Rungnir, had grown dissatisfied with his treatment by the Shadovar. He immediately sent a team up to Rungnir's glacier to meet the giant, only to find that Rungnir had already been driven into hiding. They then helped him crush Archwizard Tharno in revenge, leading to Rungnir's clan following him in rebellion and further weakening the Netherese forces.
